# Quản lý các vấn đề ở trẻ sơ sinh – WHO 2003

## Phạm vi

### Đối tượng và bối cảnh sử dụng
Tài liệu dành cho bác sĩ, điều dưỡng, hộ sinh và nhân viên y tế tại tuyến chuyển viện đầu tiên, đặc biệt ở bối cảnh nguồn lực hạn chế, khi chăm sóc trẻ sơ sinh bệnh hoặc nhỏ trong những tuần đầu đời. Hướng dẫn tổ chức theo **dấu hiệu lâm sàng** thay vì theo tên bệnh, nhằm ưu tiên nhận diện tình trạng đe dọa tính mạng, ổn định trẻ trước, sau đó mới hoàn thiện chẩn đoán và xử trí đặc hiệu.

## Đánh giá nhanh và xử trí ngay

### Dấu hiệu cấp cứu phải xử trí ngay
Trẻ phải được đánh giá ngay khi đến cơ sở. Dấu hiệu cấp cứu trong nguồn gồm: **không thở dù đã kích thích; thở ngáp; nhịp thở <20 lần/phút; chảy máu; hoặc sốc**. Sốc được mô tả bằng da nhợt, lạnh khi chạm, nhịp tim **>180 lần/phút**, trẻ cực kỳ lừ đừ hoặc bất tỉnh. Trẻ có bất kỳ dấu hiệu này có thể tử vong trong vài phút nếu chậm xử trí.

### Nếu trẻ không thở, thở ngáp hoặc RR <20/phút
- Đặt trẻ trên bề mặt ấm, dưới nguồn sưởi nếu có.
- **Hồi sức bằng bóng-mask ngay**.
- Sau ổn định ban đầu, tiếp tục đánh giá toàn diện để tìm nguyên nhân và vấn đề phối hợp.

### Nếu trẻ đang co giật hoặc co thắt
Xử trí dấu hiệu cấp cứu khác trước. Sau đó xử trí ban đầu co giật/co thắt rồi tiếp tục đánh giá. Đồng thời kiểm tra glucose máu vì hạ đường huyết là nguyên nhân quan trọng và có thể điều trị được.

### Nếu trẻ chảy máu
- Cầm chảy máu nhìn thấy được; ví dụ kẹp/buộc lại rốn nếu chảy từ cuống rốn, hoặc ép trực tiếp vị trí tổn thương bằng gạc vô khuẩn.
- Vitamin K1 (phytomenadione) **1 mg IV**, hoặc **IM nếu chưa có đường IV**.
- Lấy máu định nhóm/cross-match và hemoglobin.
- Tiếp tục xử trí chảy máu theo mức độ và nguyên nhân.

### Nếu sốc và nghi do mất máu
- Normal saline hoặc Ringer lactate **10 mL/kg trong 10 phút**; có thể lặp lại một lần sau **20 phút** nếu dấu hiệu sốc còn.
- Sau đó dùng glucose 10% theo nhu cầu duy trì theo tuổi.
- Nguồn 2003 hướng dẫn truyền ngay máu nhóm O Rh âm khi cần.
- Cho oxy lưu lượng cao và bảo đảm giữ ấm.

### Nếu sốc không nghĩ do mất máu
Truyền dịch IV **20 mL/kg trong giờ đầu**, sau đó duy trì theo tuổi; giữ ấm và xử trí sepsis. Đây là phác đồ của nguồn WHO 2003 và cần được đọc trong bối cảnh lịch sử của tài liệu.

## Đánh giá hô hấp

### Ngưỡng cần chú ý
Khi khám toàn diện, nhịp thở **>60 hoặc <30 lần/phút**, thở rên hoặc co kéo lồng ngực là dấu hiệu khó thở. Nguồn mô tả nhịp thở bình thường của trẻ sơ sinh khoảng **30–60 lần/phút**. Ngừng thở tự phát **>20 giây** được xem là apnea; nếu xảy ra, kích thích bằng xoa lưng khoảng 10 giây, và nếu trẻ không thở trở lại ngay thì hồi sức bóng-mask.

### Phân loại khó thở trong nguồn 2003
- RR **>90/phút + thở rên/co kéo** → nặng.
- RR **>90/phút không thở rên/co kéo** → vừa.
- RR **60–90/phút + thở rên/co kéo** → vừa.
- RR **60–90/phút không thở rên/co kéo** → nhẹ.
Đây là hệ phân loại của tài liệu 2003; khi có hướng dẫn WHO mới hơn trong kho, cần ưu tiên nguồn mới.

### Khó thở nặng
Đặt sonde dạ dày để giảm khí/dịch trong dạ dày, xử trí sepsis, theo dõi nhịp thở/co kéo/thở rên/apnea mỗi 3 giờ trong thời gian cần oxy và thêm 24 giờ sau ngừng oxy. Nếu nặng lên hoặc tím trung tâm, nguồn hướng dẫn tăng oxy và chuyển tuyến khẩn nếu tím vẫn tồn tại dù oxy cao.

### RDS ở trẻ nhỏ
Nguồn mô tả respiratory distress syndrome (RDS) thường gặp ở trẻ **<2,5 kg hoặc <37 tuần**, khởi phát trong vài giờ sau sinh; nặng lên trong 2 ngày đầu, ổn định 1–2 ngày tiếp theo rồi cải thiện trong 4–7 ngày. Với trẻ nhỏ có apnea, kangaroo mother care (KMC) được khuyến khích khi thích hợp vì mẹ có thể quan sát trẻ sát hơn.

## Sepsis và nhiều dấu hiệu phối hợp

### Vì sao cần nghĩ đến sepsis
Một vấn đề có thể tạo nhiều dấu hiệu và một dấu hiệu có thể do nhiều bệnh. Sepsis, ngạt, trẻ nhỏ và giang mai bẩm sinh có thể chồng lấp biểu hiện. Nếu không loại trừ được sepsis hoặc chẩn đoán khác chưa chắc chắn, tài liệu khuyên phải nghĩ đến sepsis và xử trí phù hợp.

### Yếu tố gợi ý sepsis sớm
Tiền sử mẹ có nhiễm trùng tử cung hoặc sốt từ lúc bắt đầu chuyển dạ đến **3 ngày sau sinh**, hoặc **vỡ ối >18 giờ trước sinh**, làm tăng nghi ngờ sepsis ở trẻ có triệu chứng trong những ngày đầu. Khởi phát bệnh từ ngày 4 trở đi cũng nghiêng về sepsis hơn so với ngạt chu sinh.

### Yếu tố gợi ý ngạt
Khó/chuyển dạ kéo dài, suy thai, trẻ không tự thở sau sinh và cần hồi sức làm tăng khả năng ngạt. Tuy nhiên ngạt và sepsis có thể cùng tồn tại; không dùng một dấu hiệu đơn lẻ để loại trừ bệnh còn lại.

### Hạ đường huyết trong trẻ có nhiều dấu hiệu
Nếu glucose máu **<45 mg/dL (2,6 mmol/L)**, điều trị hạ đường huyết trước rồi đánh giá lại các dấu hiệu còn tồn tại.

### Kháng sinh trong nghi sepsis
Nếu không nghi viêm màng não, nguồn 2003 dùng **ampicillin + gentamicin IV** theo tuổi/cân nặng. Nếu cải thiện sau 3 ngày: cấy máu âm tính có thể ngừng sau 5 ngày; nếu không thể cấy hoặc cấy dương, hoàn tất 10 ngày. Liều cụ thể phụ thuộc bảng tuổi/cân nặng của tài liệu gốc và không được suy diễn thêm ở đây.

## Co giật và co thắt

### Trước khi dùng thuốc chống co giật
Đo glucose. Nếu **<45 mg/dL (2,6 mmol/L)**, điều trị hạ đường huyết trước để loại trừ nguyên nhân có thể đảo ngược.

### Phenobarbital và phenytoin trong nguồn 2003
Nếu đang co giật hoặc có cơn trong 1 giờ trước: phenobarbital **20 mg/kg IV chậm trong 5 phút**; nếu chưa có IV, **20 mg/kg IM**. Nếu không dừng sau 30 phút, thêm **10 mg/kg IV/IM**; có thể lặp lại một lần sau 30 phút nữa. Nếu còn hoặc tái phát trong 6 giờ, nguồn dùng phenytoin **20 mg/kg IV**, pha trong **15 mL normal saline**, truyền **0,5 mL/phút trong 30 phút**.

### Cảnh báo diazepam trong nguồn
Nguồn WHO 2003 cảnh báo **không dùng diazepam cùng phenobarbital** cho co giật sơ sinh vì tăng nguy cơ suy tuần hoàn và suy hô hấp.

## Bất thường thân nhiệt

### Hạ thân nhiệt nặng
Làm ấm ngay bằng radiant warmer đã được làm nóng sẵn hoặc phương pháp làm ấm khác; bỏ quần áo ướt/lạnh; xử trí sepsis; đo glucose và điều trị nếu **<45 mg/dL**. Theo dõi dấu hiệu cấp cứu và nhiệt độ mỗi giờ. Nguồn coi tốc độ tăng **≥0,5°C/giờ trong 3 giờ** là dấu hiệu làm ấm thành công.

### Hạ thân nhiệt mức vừa
Nếu mẹ có mặt và trẻ không có vấn đề khác, ưu tiên **da kề da**. Nếu không thể, dùng quần áo ấm, mũ, chăn hoặc nguồn sưởi. Tăng cường bú mẹ; đo glucose; theo dõi nhiệt độ mỗi giờ trong 3 giờ và đánh giá sepsis nếu không cải thiện.

### Tăng thân nhiệt
Nguồn 2003 khuyến cáo **không dùng thuốc hạ sốt chỉ để hạ nhiệt độ trẻ**. Nếu do môi trường nóng, đưa trẻ về môi trường khoảng **25–28°C**. Nếu thân nhiệt **>39°C**, có thể lau/tắm 10–15 phút bằng nước thấp hơn thân nhiệt trẻ khoảng **4°C**, không dùng nước lạnh. Nếu nhiệt độ vẫn bất thường sau 2 giờ, xử trí sepsis. Đây là hướng dẫn lịch sử và cần ưu tiên hướng dẫn mới hơn khi có.

## Vàng da

### Vàng da sớm là dấu hiệu nghiêm trọng
Bất kỳ vàng da nhìn thấy được **trong ngày đầu đời** được xem là nghiêm trọng. Nguồn phân loại: ngày 2 vàng đến tay/chân là nghiêm trọng; từ ngày 3 trở đi vàng lòng bàn tay/lòng bàn chân là nghiêm trọng.

### Ngưỡng quang trị liệu và thay máu của nguồn 2003
Các ngưỡng dưới đây là **lịch sử của WHO 2003** và không được dùng để thay thế biểu đồ/khuyến nghị hiện hành nếu có nguồn cập nhật hơn:
- **Ngày 1:** bất kỳ vàng da nhìn thấy → quang trị liệu; ngưỡng thay máu: trẻ đủ tháng khỏe 15 mg/dL (260 micromol/L), có yếu tố nguy cơ 13 mg/dL (220 micromol/L).
- **Ngày 2:** quang trị liệu 15/260 ở trẻ đủ tháng khỏe, 13/220 nếu có nguy cơ; thay máu 25/425 và 15/260 tương ứng.
- **Ngày 3:** quang trị liệu 18/310 hoặc 16/270; thay máu 30/510 hoặc 20/340.
- **Ngày 4 trở đi:** quang trị liệu 20/340 hoặc 17/290; thay máu 30/510 hoặc 20/340.
Yếu tố nguy cơ gồm cân nặng sinh **<2,5 kg**, tuổi thai **<37 tuần**, tan máu hoặc sepsis.

### Tan máu và thiếu máu trong vàng da
Nguồn xem Hb **<13 g/dL** là dấu hiệu hỗ trợ trong bối cảnh vàng da tan máu; nếu Hb **<10 g/dL**, hướng dẫn truyền máu. Sau xuất viện, đo Hb hằng tuần trong 4 tuần; nếu **<8 g/dL**, nguồn khuyến nghị truyền máu. Các ngưỡng này thuộc tài liệu 2003.

## Dấu hiệu và lĩnh vực khác trong handbook

### Các vấn đề được bao phủ
Handbook còn có chương về trẻ nhỏ/nhẹ cân, khó bú, nôn/chướng bụng, tiêu chảy, chảy máu/nhợt, tổn thương da-niêm, nhiễm trùng rốn, viêm mắt, chấn thương sinh, dị tật bẩm sinh, trẻ không triệu chứng có mẹ mắc viêm gan B/lao/đái tháo đường/giang mai, và mẹ nhiễm HIV. Phần nguyên tắc bao gồm giữ ấm, nuôi dưỡng/dịch, oxy, kháng sinh, phòng nhiễm trùng, truyền máu, tiêm chủng, tăng trưởng, hỗ trợ tâm lý, chuyển tuyến, xuất viện và theo dõi.

## Quy tắc ưu tiên cho CareBridge

### Đây là nguồn lịch sử
Tài liệu xuất bản năm **2003**. Với hồi sức sơ sinh, oxy, sepsis, vàng da, KMC, thời điểm xuất viện và các can thiệp đã có hướng dẫn WHO mới hơn trong kho, RAG phải ưu tiên nguồn mới hơn và chỉ dùng tài liệu này để bổ sung bối cảnh hoặc khi không có hướng dẫn cập nhật hơn.

## PHỤ LỤC FULL-TEXT CHUẨN HÓA TỪ PDF GỐC

### Mục đích bảo toàn tri thức

Phần dưới giữ nội dung chuyên môn của PDF gốc sau khi loại số trang, header/footer lặp, boilerplate xuất bản và danh mục tài liệu tham khảo. Nội dung được tuyến tính hóa để tránh bảng nhiều cột bị gãy khi chunking. Các khuyến nghị/liều/ngưỡng vẫn thuộc đúng phiên bản của nguồn; nếu file có metadata `historical_source` hoặc có guideline mới hơn thì phải áp dụng quy tắc ưu tiên phiên bản ở phần đầu file.

## Section 4: Appendix

### Section 4: Appendix — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: Record Keeping; A-1

- Dòng dữ liệu: Essential Equipment, Supplies, and Drugs; A-9

- Dòng dữ liệu: Index; A-15

- Dòng dữ liệu: iv; Table of contents

- Dòng dữ liệu: vi; Foreword

- Dòng dữ liệu: PREFACE; vii

This guide has been produced by the World Health Organization to assist countries with limited resources in their efforts to reduce neonatal mortality and to ensure care for newborn babies with problems due to complications of pregnancy and childbirth, such as asphyxia, sepsis, and low birth weight or preterm birth. The main section of this guide is arranged by clinical signs or findings, which facilitates early identification of illness, and provides up-to-date guidelines

### Section 4: Appendix — phần 2

for clinical management. Use of these guidelines is essential in promoting and assessing the quality of health services and training providers and supporting quality services through supervision and feedback on performance. The interventions in this guide are based on the latest available scientific evidence and the guide will be updated as new information is acquired. In addition, the diagnostic and management guidelines in this guide are

consistent with the other WHO materials in the Integrated Management of Pregnancy and Childbirth (IMPAC) series, including Pregnancy, Childbirth, Postpartum and Newborn Care: A Guide for Essential Practice and Managing Complications in Pregnancy and Childbirth: A Guide for Midwives and Doctors. This guide is complementary to the Integrated Management of Childhood Illness (IMCI) guidelines for care of the sick young infant: the major illnesses emphasized in this guide originate at birth or

### Section 4: Appendix — phần 3

during the first week of life, while the illnesses covered in the IMCI guidelines generally originate after that period. It is hoped that this guide will be readily available whenever a doctor, nurse, or midwife is confronted with a sick or small newborn baby. In addition, all of the guides in the IMPAC series can be used by national ministries of health to help ensure that countries have state-of-the-art information upon which to

base their national policy standards, pre-service training, and service delivery guidelines. For the guide to be fully effective, its users should also be trained in a setting where skills can be practised. Many training packages and job aids are available to accompany the guides in the IMPAC series.

- Dòng dữ liệu: viii; Preface

- Dòng dữ liệu: INTRODUCTION; ix

### Section 4: Appendix — phần 4

A newborn baby who is small or has a potentially life-threatening problem is in an emergency situation requiring immediate diagnosis and management. Delay in identification of the problem or in providing the correct management may be fatal. This guide provides up-to-date, authoritative clinical guidelines for use at the first referral level in low-resource settings by the doctors, nurses, midwives, and other health care workers who are responsible for the

care of newborn babies with problems during the first week(s) of life. The guide can also be used to identify less common conditions that require referral to a higher level. To effectively apply these guidelines for care of the sick or small newborn baby, a general medical officer and nurses with skills in caring for newborn babies should be available at the hospital 24 hours per day. In addition, basic support systems are necessary, including:

### Section 4: Appendix — phần 5

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Basic laboratory capabilities for measurement of haemoglobin or

haematocrit (erythrocyte volume fraction), blood glucose, and serum bilirubin, as well as culture and sensitivity of blood, pus, and cerebrospinal fluid;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Selected essential drugs, including key antibiotics such as ampicillin and

gentamicin;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Essential equipment and supplies, including accurate weighing scales and

a microdropper for infusions;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Capability to provide safe blood transfusion.

In certain settings, these requirements may not be available; this guide allows for these situations and provides alternative methods of assessment or management where possible. However, all health care workers and policy- makers are encouraged to strive for wider availability of these basic standards to enable effective care of sick and small newborn babies.

## How To Use The Guide

### How To Use The Guide — phần 1

The emphasis of the guide is on rapid assessment and decision-making, in order to prioritize the sickest babies and the most urgent actions.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The first priority is to immediately assess all babies for emergency (life-

threatening) signs and identify those who require immediate management.

- Dòng dữ liệu: x; Introduction

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A further assessment, including history and a complete examination, is

then necessary to guide the health care worker in identifying appropriate management for the specific problem(s) identified. The main text of the guide is arranged primarily by clinical signs or findings (e.g. breathing difficulty). Because this approach is different from most medical texts, which are arranged by disease categories, a list of diagnoses with the page number of the corresponding diagnosis table is provided. The

### How To Use The Guide — phần 2

guide comprises four sections, each numbered separately and designated with a letter code. Cross-referencing is used extensively throughout the text to allow the reader to quickly find the relevant information in all sections of the manual.

## Section 1, Assessment, Findings, and Management (designated by the

### Section 1, Assessment, Findings, and Management (designated by the — phần 1

letter “F” in page numbers), contains a short chapter used to identify those babies at risk of dying very soon and provides initial guidance on immediate management necessary to stabilize the baby’s condition. This section also includes a description of the further assessment necessary to identify the baby’s specific problem(s) and includes relevant history questions and a complete physical examination. A table guides the health care worker through

the examination, provides guidance on initial management when necessary, and then directs the health care worker to the most appropriate chapter(s) for management of the specific problem(s). The following chapters, with a few exceptions, explore each sign or finding separately. Most chapters begin with general management (where appropriate) followed by a differential diagnosis table that guides the health care worker to the most

### Section 1, Assessment, Findings, and Management (designated by the — phần 2

probable diagnosis that is causing the problem. The findings from the history, examination, and laboratory investigations (or other known diagnoses) are listed separately in each table. To help the user determine the significance of the possible findings, italics and bold text are used to distinguish between findings: a diagnosis cannot be made if a finding listed in bold text is absent in the baby. The presence of a finding listed in bold, however, does not

### Section 1, Assessment, Findings, and Management (designated by the — phần 3

guarantee the diagnosis. The diagnosis is definitively confirmed if a finding listed in italicized text is present. Findings in plain text are supportive findings; their presence helps to confirm the diagnosis, but their absence cannot be used to rule out the diagnosis. Simplified management protocols follow the diagnosis tables. Where there are several choices of therapy, the most effective and inexpensive is chosen. Clear guidance on drugs and dosages, as well as alternatives, is provided.

Conditions requiring referral to a higher level are included in the examination table and in individual chapters where appropriate.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Introduction; xi

## Section 2, Principles of Newborn Baby Care (designated by the letter “C”

### Section 2, Principles of Newborn Baby Care (designated by the letter “C” — phần 1

in page numbers), outlines the general principles of managing sick or small newborn babies. This section includes the general principles of ongoing care, including feeding, maintaining normal body temperature, preventing infection, giving immunizations, and assessing growth. Other chapters provide guidance on giving oxygen, antibiotics, and blood transfusion. Guidance on emotional support, visitation, and discharge and follow-up is also included.

## Section 3, Procedures (designated by the letter “P” in page numbers),

### Section 3, Procedures (designated by the letter “P” in page numbers), — phần 1

describes the procedures that may be necessary in the care of the sick or small baby. These procedures are not intended to be detailed “how-to” instructions, but rather a summary of the main steps associated with each procedure. Because the general principles of care are summarized in Section 2, these are not repeated for each procedure unless specific to the procedure.

## Section 4, Appendix (designated by the letter “A” in page numbers),

### Section 4, Appendix (designated by the letter “A” in page numbers), — phần 1

contains sample records and a list of essential equipment, supplies, and drugs. An index is included and is organized so that it can be used in an emergency situation to find relevant material quickly. The most critical information, including diagnosis, management, and relevant procedure(s), is listed first in bold. Other entries follow in alphabetical order. Only the pages containing critical or relevant information are included, rather than listing every page that contains the word or phrase.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Table C-10; Acceptable antiseptic and disinfectant solutions; C-41

- Dòng dữ liệu: Table C-11; Guidelines for processing instruments and equipment; C-43

- Dòng dữ liệu: Table C-12; Sample cleaning schedule for the newborn special care unit C-44

### Section 2: Principles Of Newborn Baby Care — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: Table C-13; Suggested equipment, supplies, drugs, and fluid for

- Dòng dữ liệu: transfer of a sick or small baby; C-64

## Small Newborn Baby

### Small Newborn Baby — phần 1

Whether babies who need care are brought to the health care facility from home, transferred from another institution or ward, or brought from the delivery room as a result of a complicated birth, managing their care involves a cycle of planning, implementing, and evaluating care based on ongoing assessment of the baby’s condition. The care that the baby receives at the health care facility is divided into several steps, as described below.

Follow infection prevention principles and practices (page C-37) at all times when examining and treating babies, especially if the baby has diarrhoea or a possible infection of the skin, eye, or umbilicus.

This guide provides for care of a sick or small baby in two situations:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The baby has been observed in a health care facility since birth: The

### Small Newborn Baby — phần 2

course of the problem after birth is known, and reliable information about the baby, as well as the history of the mother, pregnancy, and birth, are available.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The baby was admitted from home: There is no available or reliable

information about the baby’s condition from birth to the present time, and/or the history of the mother, pregnancy, and birth is unavailable or unreliable. Very often these babies will be seen at an advanced stage of illness.

## Arrival, Rapid Assessment, And Immediate Management

### Arrival, Rapid Assessment, And Immediate Management — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Instruct staff to call a health care provider as soon as a baby less than one

week old is brought to the facility. Do not let the baby wait to receive care.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the admission and reception area is organized so that every

baby can be seen quickly.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Assess all sick or small babies before doing any of the usual

administrative procedures to admit the baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Immediately upon the baby’s arrival, assess the baby for emergency signs

that indicate that the baby is in critical condition and at risk of dying within minutes (page F-5):

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; While looking for emergency signs, introduce yourself to the mother

and ask her (or whoever brought the baby in):

- Dòng dữ liệu: F-2; Organizing care of the sick or small newborn baby

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; What is wrong with the baby?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; When did the problem(s) first start?

### Arrival, Rapid Assessment, And Immediate Management — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; What are the names of the mother and baby?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; How old is the baby?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Was the baby brought in from outside the health care facility?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Keep the baby with the mother, if possible, and allow her to be

present during the assessment and for any procedures, if appropriate.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide immediate management for any life-threatening emergency

signs, as directed in Table F-1 (page F-6), before continuing with the further assessment.

Give priority to stabilizing the sick or small baby before assessing and treating the underlying cause of the problem.

## Further Assessment And Management

### Further Assessment And Management — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Once the baby has received immediate management, obtain the history of

the mother and the baby. Then use Table F-2 (page F-11) to complete a thorough examination to determine the underlying problem(s), and admit the baby (page F-21), if necessary.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide specific management for the problem(s) identified, if necessary,

following the guidelines in Table F-2 (page F-11) to determine which chapter(s) to use.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Arrange for transfer and referral (page C-63), if necessary.

## Ongoing Care

### Ongoing Care — phần 1

In addition to specific care for the baby’s problem(s), provide general and ongoing care.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Develop a general plan of care that takes into account the special needs

of the baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Monitor the baby’s progress and recovery by performing ongoing general

assessments (group assessments together to disturb the baby as little as possible) including:

- Dòng dữ liệu: Organizing care of the sick or small newborn baby; F-3

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; respiratory rate;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; heart rate;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; colour;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; temperature;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; weight;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; rate and volume of IV fluid;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; frequency and volume of feeds.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Be prepared to change the plan of care according to changes in the

### Ongoing Care — phần 2

baby’s condition, determined from the findings of the ongoing general assessments and any other specific assessments required for the particular problem.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide emotional support to the mother and other family members

(page C-57).

## Documenting Care

### Documenting Care — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Record the necessary treatment in a written plan of care, and

communicate this plan to the medical team and other staff involved in the care of the baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Document any changes in the baby’s condition, and communicate them

to appropriate staff.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that information is communicated between on-call medical

officers and new staff on different shifts.

## Discharge And Follow-Up

### Discharge And Follow-Up — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Follow guidance in the individual problem chapters in determining when

to discharge the baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Plan the discharge:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Provide any immunizations necessary (page C-51);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Provide instructions for general home care;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Advise on breastfeeding, and ensure that the baby is feeding well.

- Dòng dữ liệu: F-4; Organizing care of the sick or small newborn baby

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Discharge the baby (page C-67) and schedule follow-up visits, if

necessary, for specific conditions and to monitor feeding and growth.

## Rapid Assessment And Immediate Management F-5

### Rapid Assessment And Immediate Management F-5 — phần 1

Assess every baby for emergency signs as soon as the baby arrives, regardless of whether the baby is coming from another ward in the health care facility, is transferred from another health care facility, or is brought from home. A few babies may have emergency signs that indicate a problem that is so serious the baby may die within minutes if not immediately treated. Use this chapter to rapidly assess babies for emergency signs and provide immediate management.

Examine the baby immediately for the following emergency signs, and provide immediate management (Table F-1, page F-6) if found:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Not breathing at all, even when stimulated; gasping; or

respiratory rate less than 20 breaths per minute; OR

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Bleeding; OR

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Shock (pallor, cold to the touch, heart rate more than 180

beats per minute, extremely lethargic or unconscious).

## Rapid Assessment

### Rapid Assessment — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place the baby on a warm surface under a radiant warmer and under good

light.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Immediately resuscitate the baby using a bag and mask (page P-1) if the

baby:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; is not breathing at all, even when stimulated; OR

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; is gasping; OR

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; has a respiratory rate less than 20 breaths per minute.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby is having a convulsion or spasm, treat any emergency

sign(s) first. Then provide initial management of the convulsion or spasm (page F-59) before continuing with the further assessment.

## Immediate Management

### Immediate Management — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Weigh the baby (page C-53).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Establish an IV line (page P-21).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide immediate management (Table F-1, page F-6).

- Dòng dữ liệu: F-6; Rapid assessment and immediate management

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Once immediate management has been completed, continue with the

further assessment (page F-7).

- Dòng dữ liệu: TABLE F-1; Immediate management of emergency signs

- Dòng dữ liệu: Emergency Sign; Immediate Management

- Dòng dữ liệu: • Not breathing at all, even; 1. Resuscitate the baby using a bag and mask (page

- Dòng dữ liệu: when stimulated; OR; P-1).

- Dòng dữ liệu: • Gasping; OR; 2. Give oxygen (page C-25) at a high flow rate.

• Respiratory rate less than 20 breaths per minute

- Dòng dữ liệu: • Bleeding; 1. Stop visible bleeding, if possible (e.g. if the bleeding

### Immediate Management — phần 2

is from the umbilicus, reclamp or retie the umbilical stump; if the bleeding is from a cut or male circumcision site, press on the bleeding site with a sterile compress). 2. Give vitamin K1 (phytomenadione) 1 mg IV (or IM if an IV line has not yet been established). 3. Take a blood sample (page P-9) to type and cross- match, and measure haemoglobin. 4. Provide general management of bleeding (page F-114).

- Dòng dữ liệu: • Shock; If bleeding is the likely cause of shock:

1. Infuse normal saline or Ringer’s lactate 10 ml/kg body weight over 10 minutes, and repeat once after 20 minutes if signs of shock continue. Then infuse 10% glucose at maintenance volume according to the baby’s age (Table C-4, page C-22). 2. Immediately give a blood transfusion (page P-31) using type O, Rh-negative blood. 3. Give oxygen at a high flow rate (page C-25). 4. Ensure warmth (page C-1). If bleeding is not the likely cause of shock:

### Immediate Management — phần 3

1. Infuse IV fluid 20 ml/kg body weight over the first hour, and then continue IV fluid at maintenance volume according to the baby’s age (Table C-4, page C-22). 2. Ensure warmth (page C-1). 3. Treat for sepsis (page F-41).

## Further Assessment And Management F-7

### Further Assessment And Management F-7 — phần 1

After examining for emergency signs (i.e. not breathing, gasping, respiratory rate less than 20 breaths per minute, bleeding, or shock) and providing immediate management (Table F-1, page F-6), continue to assess the baby and make a list of findings.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Obtain the history of the baby and the mother (below).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Examine the baby completely (Table F-2, page F-11).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use the findings from the history and examination to choose the most

appropriate chapter(s) in this section of the guide.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Complete additional examinations, if necessary, and determine the

required laboratory investigations as directed in the chapter(s) in this

## section of the guide.

### section of the guide. — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Perform appropriate laboratory investigations, and treat the baby (and/or

the mother or her partner(s), if necessary).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Record all information, including:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; the findings of the history, examination, and laboratory investigations;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; treatment given;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; changes in the baby’s condition.

## History

### History — phần 1

Review the referral notes or records of the birth, if available. Ask the following questions about the mother and baby and use the answers, together with the findings of the examination and laboratory investigations, to determine the probable diagnosis.

BABY Ask the mother (or whoever brought the baby in):

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; What is the problem? Why is the baby here?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; What kind of care, including specific treatment, has the baby already

received?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; How old is the baby?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; How much did the baby weigh at birth?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Was the baby born at term? If not, at how many weeks gestation was the

- Dòng dữ liệu: F-8; Further assessment and management

baby born?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where was the baby born? Did anybody assist the birth?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; How was the baby immediately after birth?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Did the baby spontaneously breathe at birth?

### History — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Did the baby require resuscitation? If so, what was the length of time

before spontaneous breathing was established?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Did the baby move and cry normally?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; When did the problem first start?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Has the baby’s condition changed since the problem was first noted? Is

the problem getting worse? If so, how rapidly and in what way?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Is the baby having problems feeding, including any of the following?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; poor or no feeding since birth or after a period of feeding normally;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; coughing or choking during feeding;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; vomiting after a feeding.

## Mother

### Mother — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Review the mother’s medical, obstetric, and social history.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask the mother if she has any questions or concerns (e.g. special

concerns or anxiety about breastfeeding).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the mother is not present, determine where she is, what her condition

is, and whether she will be able to care for the baby, including breastfeeding or expressing breast milk.

## Pregnancy

### Pregnancy — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask the mother the following questions regarding her pregnancy:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; What was the duration of your pregnancy?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Did you have any chronic diseases during the pregnancy, including

hepatitis B, tuberculosis, diabetes, or syphilis (symptomatic or seropositive)?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Do you know your HIV status? If so, can you tell me?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Did you have any complications during your pregnancy? If so, what,

if any, treatment did you receive?

- Dòng dữ liệu: Further assessment and management; F-9

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the mother has hepatitis B, tuberculosis, diabetes, or syphilis,

complete the examination in Table F-2 (page F-11), and treat any specific problem(s) the baby has. If the baby is asymptomatic (no findings of illness), see page F-155 for appropriate treatment based on the mother’s problem.

### Pregnancy — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the mother is HIV positive, complete the examination in Table F-2

(page F-11), and treat any specific problem(s) the baby has. Then see page F-159 for appropriate treatment based on the mother’s problem.

## Labour And Birth

### Labour And Birth — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask the mother the following questions about her labour and birth:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Did you develop any complications, such as uterine infection or

fever any time from the onset of labour to three days after birth?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Were your membranes ruptured for more than 18 hours before birth?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Was the labour or birth difficult or complicated, including any of the

following?

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; fetal distress;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; prolonged labour;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; caesarean section;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; instrumental vaginal delivery (e.g. forceps or vacuum

extraction);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; malposition or malpresentation of the baby (e.g. breech);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; any other complications.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Did you develop any complications after the birth?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the mother had a uterine infection or fever any time from the onset

### Labour And Birth — phần 2

of labour to three days after birth, or rupture of membranes for more than 18 hours before birth, continue taking the history, complete the examination (Table F-2, page F-11), and treat any specific problem(s). Then see page F-55 for appropriate treatment based on the mother’s problem.

- Dòng dữ liệu: F-10; Further assessment and management

## Examination

### Examination — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Continue any immediate management that was started for an emergency

sign (not breathing, gasping, respiratory rate less than 20 breaths per minute, bleeding, or shock; Table F-1, page F-6). If the baby develops an emergency sign during the examination, return to Table F-1 for immediate management, and proceed with the examination once the baby’s condition is stable.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Examine the baby as directed in Table F-2 (page F-11):

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Examine the baby under a radiant warmer unless it is clear that the

baby has been overheated;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Allow the mother to be present during the examination;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby has not been weighed yet, weigh the baby (page C-53),

and record the weight;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; While talking to the mother and before undressing the baby, observe

the baby for:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; colour;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; respiratory rate;

### Examination — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; posture;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; movement;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; reaction to stimuli;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; obvious abnormalities.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; As you proceed in the examination, explain the findings to the

mother in simple terms and point out abnormalities (page C-57). Obtain informed consent before performing an invasive procedure;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; A newborn baby can have more than one problem. While performing

the examination, provide only the treatment specifically listed in the following table (i.e. after the statement “ACT NOW”). Wait until the entire examination is complete before beginning specific management of the baby’s problems, treating the problems designated as priorities first.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Further assessment and management; F-11

- Dòng dữ liệu: TABLE F-2; Examination of the newborn baby

- Dòng dữ liệu: Look at; Look for; Immediate Action(s) Required and

• Chest indrawing (Fig. F-3, page F-47)

- Dòng dữ liệu: cessation of breathing; breathe by rubbing the baby’s

- Dòng dữ liệu: seconds); does not begin to breathe

immediately, resuscitate the baby using a bag and mask (page P-1).

For management of apnoea, see page F-52.

### Examination — phần 4

The normal respiratory rate of a newborn baby is 30 to 60 breaths per minute with no chest indrawing or grunting on expiration; however, small babies (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks gestation) may have some mild chest indrawing, and it is not abnormal for a baby to periodically stop breathing for a few seconds. When determining the respiratory rate, count the number of breaths taken during a full minute, as babies may

breathe irregularly (up to 80 breaths per minute) for short periods of time. If unsure of the respiratory rate, repeat the count.

- Dòng dữ liệu: COLOUR; • Pallor; For management of pallor and

possible bleeding, see page F-113.

- Dòng dữ liệu: • Jaundice (yellow); For management of jaundice, see

page F-77.

- Dòng dữ liệu: • Central cyanosis (blue; ACT NOW: Give oxygen at a high

- Dòng dữ liệu: tongue and lips; note; flow rate (page C-25).

that blue skin in

- Dòng dữ liệu: addition to blue tongue; For management of central cyanosis,

- Dòng dữ liệu: and lips indicates a; see page F-47.

very serious problem)

Babies born at term appear paler than preterm babies because their skin is thicker.

- Dòng dữ liệu: stethoscope); minute; abnormal body temperature,

bleeding, breathing difficulty).

### Examination — phần 6

The normal heart rate of a newborn baby is 100 to 160 beats per minute, but it is not uncommon for the heart rate to be more than 160 beats per minute for short periods of time during the first few days of life, especially if the baby is distressed. If unsure of the heart rate, repeat the count.

To classify and manage low body temperature, see page F-69. Management of a body temperature less than 32 °C is a priority once the examination is complete (page F-71).

Additional management of a baby with convulsions/spasms or a history of convulsions/spasms is a priority once the examination is complete (page F-59).

- Dòng dữ liệu: • Jitteriness (rapid and; During the examination, look for

- Dòng dữ liệu: repetitive movements; other, more specific signs. If more

- Dòng dữ liệu: that are caused by; specific signs are not found, see

baby or loud noises and can be stopped by cuddling, feeding, or flexing a limb)

### Examination — phần 9

The normal resting posture of a term newborn baby includes loosely clenched fists and flexed arms, hips, and knees (Fig. F-1B, page F-23). The limbs may be extended in small babies (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks gestation; Fig. F-1A, page F-23). Babies who were in a breech position may have fully flexed hips and knees, and the feet may be near the mouth; alternatively, the legs and feet may be to the side of the baby (Fig. F-12, page F-146).

- Dòng dữ liệu: F-14; Further assessment and management

- Dòng dữ liệu: painful procedures); F-35). Management of the cause of

unconsciousness is a priority once the examination is complete. The normal newborn baby ranges from quiet to alert and is consolable when upset. The baby is arousable when quiet or asleep.

### Examination — phần 14

Some skin conditions are common and should not cause concern as long as the baby is otherwise normal. These include milia (white spots around the nose), typically seen on day 1 or later, and erythema toxicum (red spots with tiny white centres), seen on the face, trunk, and back on day 2 or later. In addition, it is not abnormal for the baby’s skin on the trunk, abdomen, and back to peel after the first day.

- Dòng dữ liệu: swollen, draining pus,; the umbilicus, see page F-135.

- Dòng dữ liệu: umbilicus; umbilical cord, if necessary.

For management of bleeding, see page F-113.

The normal umbilicus is bluish-white in colour on day 1. It then begins to dry and shrink and falls off after 7 to 10 days.

It is normal for a baby to have six to eight watery stools per day. Vaginal bleeding in the female newborn baby may occur for a few days during the first week of life and is not a sign of a problem.

## Immediate Actions Once Examination Is Complete

### Immediate Actions Once Examination Is Complete — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Determine appropriate management:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Categorize the findings from the examination and treat priority

findings first (temperature less than 32 °C, convulsions/spasms, unconsciousness);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Continue to treat emergency signs (i.e. respiratory rate less than 20

breaths per minute, gasping, not breathing, bleeding, or shock) and continue any treatment started during the examination (i.e. “ACT NOW”);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Note that most of the chapters that follow in this section explore

each sign or finding separately and lead to appropriate management pathways. In some cases, more than one finding of illness may be identified upon further assessment, and these “clusters” of findings may all point to a single serious problem. If the assessment reveals more than one finding, first see the chapter Multiple Findings (Most Often Sepsis or Asphyxia) (page F-35) to rule out asphyxia,

### Immediate Actions Once Examination Is Complete — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: Further assessment and management; F-21

sepsis, congenital syphilis, or problems due to small size, and then use any other chapter(s) necessary;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Keep in mind that you may be able to treat several problems at the

same time.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If a specific problem is not identified and the only finding is lethargy

or another non-specific sign (i.e. drowsiness, reduced activity, floppiness, irritability, or jitteriness), or the baby “looks ill,” see the

## chapter Lethargy and Other Non-Specific Signs (page F-87).

### chapter Lethargy and Other Non-Specific Signs (page F-87). — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Perform the following before beginning specific treatment, or as soon as

possible:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby is less than one hour old, give eye prophylaxis

(1% silver nitrate, 2.5% polyvidone iodine solution, or 1% tetracycline ointment);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Give vitamin K1 (phytomenadione) 1 mg IM once (or IV if an IV

line has already been established) if the baby has not already been given it.

The mother, who is constantly with the baby, may notice subtle changes in the baby’s condition. Listen to her comments and re-examine the baby at any time if there is concern.

## Next Steps

### Next Steps — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Review the findings with the mother and obtain informed consent before

performing additional procedures.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Continue any treatment already started.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Determine what procedures and laboratory investigations are required.

Be sure to draw enough blood at one time to perform all the necessary investigations.

## Admitting The Baby To The Health Care Facility

### Admitting The Baby To The Health Care Facility — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If treatment of the baby requires admitting the baby to the health

care facility, ensure the following:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Explain the baby’s condition to the mother and the reason(s) why the

baby needs to be admitted. Answer any questions she may have;

- Dòng dữ liệu: F-22; Further assessment and management

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Check that the baby is properly identified with a name tag on the

wrist or ankle;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Check that the umbilical cord is securely clamped/tied;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Carry out necessary administrative procedures (e.g. record keeping,

page A-1);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Care for the baby in the newborn special care unit, if one is

available:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If a newborn special care unit is not available, keep the baby

with the mother in an area that is close to the nursing station;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Keep babies with infections separate from babies with non-

### Admitting The Baby To The Health Care Facility — phần 2

infectious problems, and follow infection prevention practices at all times (page C-37);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Admit the mother, if necessary, to keep her with the baby to

allow breastfeeding or to provide expressed breast milk (page C-15). The mother does not have to be admitted if she is able to stay nearby;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the mother and baby are separated, help the mother to visit

the baby, if possible, and provide a comfortable chair for her;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the mother is not able to visit the baby, inform her of the

baby’s condition at least twice daily.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Review the list of differential diagnoses again after receiving the results

of the initial investigations (e.g. blood glucose) and after observing the baby’s response to initial treatment. Make any changes in treatment that may be necessary.

## Small Baby F-23

### Small Baby F-23 — phần 1

Preterm babies (born before 37 weeks gestation) tend to have more problems than term babies who are small (less than 2.5 kg at birth). However, because the baby’s gestational age is not always known, this guide refers to preterm babies and small term babies collectively as “small babies.” If the baby’s gestational age is known, use this, when possible, to guide diagnosis and management decisions. Remember that the more preterm or the smaller the

baby is, the more likely the baby is to have problems.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; After completing the examination in Table F-2 (page F-11), review

relevant differential diagnosis tables carefully to determine the diagnosis that most closely matches the baby’s findings, keeping in mind that a small baby can have any problem that a term baby can have. Note that a small baby:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; may have a problem that is specific to small babies (e.g. jaundice of

### Small Baby F-23 — phần 2

prematurity) but may also have any other problem that a full-size, term baby can have (e.g. jaundice associated with sepsis);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; has a different resting posture (Fig. F-1A) than a term baby (Fig.

F-1B), and this is not necessarily an indication of a problem (e.g. floppiness).

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE F-1; Normal resting posture of small (A) and term (B) babies

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Small babies are prone to complications. Some problems that small

babies are particularly susceptible to include:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; feeding difficulty (Feeding difficulty is a common problem; as the

baby grows, feeding ability improves.);

- Dòng dữ liệu: F-24; Small baby

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; abnormal body temperature (page F-69; Kangaroo mother care

### Small Baby F-23 — phần 3

[page F-30] is the recommended method of maintaining a small baby’s body temperature. This method of care is recommended for babies who do not have a serious illness, and particularly for those who weigh less than 1.8 kg.);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; breathing difficulty (page F-47), such as respiratory distress

syndrome and apnoea;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; necrotizing enterocolitis (page F-104);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; jaundice of prematurity (page F-82);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; intraventricular bleeding (page F-66);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; anaemia (page F-119; To prevent iron deficiency anaemia, give small

babies an oral iron preparation to give elemental iron 2 mg/kg body weight once daily from two months of age up to 23 months of age);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; low blood glucose (page F-91).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Because a small baby typically has multiple findings—which may be

### Small Baby F-23 — phần 4

because the baby is small or because the baby has a more severe problem—review the chapter Multiple Findings (Most Often Sepsis or Asphyxia) (page F-35), looking especially for signs of sepsis or asphyxia. Note that small babies often develop new findings during the first days and weeks of life.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Regardless of what other problems they may have, all small babies

require special considerations for feeding, fluid management, and maintenance of normal body temperature (ideally using kangaroo mother care), which are described below. In addition, review the chapters in the Newborn Care Principles section for general management instructions applicable to all newborn babies.

## General Principles Of Feeding Small Babies

### General Principles Of Feeding Small Babies — phần 1

Small babies often have difficulty feeding simply because they are not mature enough to feed well. Good feeding ability can usually be established by 34 to 35 weeks post-menstrual age. Until that time, substantial effort may be needed to ensure adequate feeding. Provide special support and attention to the mother during this difficult period.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Small baby; F-25

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain to the mother that:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; her breast milk is the best food for the baby;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; breastfeeding is especially important for a small baby;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; it may take longer for a small baby to establish breastfeeding;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; it is usually normal if the baby:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; tires easily and suckles weakly at first;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; suckles for shorter periods of time before resting;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; falls asleep during feeding;

### General Principles Of Feeding Small Babies — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; pauses for long periods between suckling.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Have the mother keep the baby at the breast for a longer period of time

and allow long pauses between suckling, or a long, slow feed. Assure the mother that breastfeeding will become easier once the baby becomes bigger.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Have the mother follow the general principles of exclusive breastfeeding

(page C-11).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the baby is fed frequently:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby weighs 1.25 to 2.5 kg, feed the baby at least eight times

in 24 hours (i.e. every three hours);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby weighs less than 1.25 kg, feed the baby at least 12 times

in 24 hours (i.e. every two hours);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Follow the guidelines in Tables F-3 to F-8 (pages F-27 to F-29) to

determine the required daily volume of feeds and/or fluid.

### General Principles Of Feeding Small Babies — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby is not suckling well enough to receive an adequate volume

of milk:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Encourage the mother to give expressed breast milk using an

alternative feeding method (page C-14);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Be sure the mother always attempts to breastfeed the baby before

offering expressed breast milk, unless the baby cannot be breastfed;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If necessary, the mother can improve the flow of milk by expressing

a small amount of milk before allowing the baby to attach to the breast.

- Dòng dữ liệu: F-26; Small baby

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the baby is receiving enough milk by assessing the baby’s

growth (page C-53).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby is not gaining weight adequately (less than 15 g/kg body

### General Principles Of Feeding Small Babies — phần 4

weight per day over three days), have the mother express breast milk (page C-15) into two different cups. Have her give the contents of the second cup, which contains more of the fat-rich hind milk, to the baby first, and then supplement with whatever is required from the first cup.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby is vomiting or has abdominal distension or episodes of

apnoea or if more than 20% of the previous feed is retained in the stomach (gastric residual) just before the next feed (when fed by gastric tube):

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Stop feeding. Establish an IV line (page P-21), and give IV fluid at

maintenance volume according to the baby’s weight and age (Tables F-3 to F-8, page F-27 to F-29) for 12 hours;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Reassess the baby after 12 hours:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby’s condition is improving, restart feeds, observing

carefully;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby’s condition is not improving, continue giving IV

### General Principles Of Feeding Small Babies — phần 5

fluid at maintenance volume for another 12 hours. Then restart feeds, giving the same volume as the last feed and observing the baby carefully.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby is being fed by gastric tube and there is an increasing

volume of gastric residuals (milk remaining in the stomach from the previous feed), suspect necrotizing enterocolitis (page F-104).

## Feed And Fluid Volumes For Small Babies

### Feed And Fluid Volumes For Small Babies — phần 1

Small babies require different feed and fluid volumes based on their condition and weight. Review the categories below to determine the appropriate total daily fluid and feed volumes for the baby.

## 1.75 To 2.5 Kg

### 1.75 To 2.5 Kg — phần 1

• Allow the baby to begin breastfeeding (page C-11). If the baby cannot be breastfed, give expressed breast milk using an alternative feeding method (page C-14). Use Table C-4 (page C-22) to determine the required volume of milk for the feed based on the baby’s age.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Small baby; F-27

## 1.5 To 1.749 Kg

### 1.5 To 1.749 Kg — phần 1

• Give expressed breast milk using an alternative feeding method (page C-14) every three hours according to Table F-3 until the baby is able to breastfeed. TABLE F-3 Volumes of breast milk for a baby weighing 1.5 to 1.749 kg without major illness

Day of Life

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1; 2; 3; 4; 5; 6; 7

- Dòng dữ liệu: Feed volume every three; 12; 18; 22; 26; 30; 33; 35

hours (ml/feed)

## 1.25 To 1.49 Kg

### 1.25 To 1.49 Kg — phần 1

• Give expressed breast milk by gastric tube (page C-18) every three hours according to Table F-4.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Progress to feeding by cup/spoon (page C-16) as soon as the baby can

swallow without coughing or spitting. TABLE F-4 Volumes of breast milk for a baby weighing 1.25 to 1.49 kg without major illness

Day of Life

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1; 2; 3; 4; 5; 6; 7

- Dòng dữ liệu: Feed volume every three; 10; 15; 18; 22; 26; 28; 30

hours (ml/feed)

## Less Than 1.25 Kg

### Less Than 1.25 Kg — phần 1

• Establish an IV line (page P-21), and give only IV fluid (according to Table F-5, page F-28) for the first 48 hours.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give expressed breast milk by gastric tube (page C-18) every two hours

starting on day 3, or later if the baby’s condition is not yet stable, and slowly decrease the volume of IV fluid while increasing the volume of oral feeds according to Table F-5 (page F-28).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Progress to feeding by cup/spoon (page C-16) as soon as the baby can

swallow without coughing or spitting.

- Dòng dữ liệu: F-28; Small baby

- Dòng dữ liệu: TABLE F-5; Volumes of IV fluid and breast milk for all babies weighing

less than 1.25 kg

Day of Life

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1; 2; 3; 4; 5; 6; 7

- Dòng dữ liệu: IV fluid rate (ml/hour or; 4; 4; 3; 3; 2; 2; 0

microdrops/minute)

- Dòng dữ liệu: Feed volume every two; 0; 0; 3; 5; 8; 11; 15

hours (ml/feed)

## 1.75 To 2.5 Kg

### 1.75 To 2.5 Kg — phần 1

• If the baby does not initially require IV fluid (according to the relevant

## chapter for the baby’s problem), allow the baby to begin breastfeeding

### chapter for the baby’s problem), allow the baby to begin breastfeeding — phần 1

(page C-11). If the baby cannot be breastfed, give expressed breast milk using an alternative feeding method (page C-14). Determine the required volume of milk for the feed based on the baby’s age (Table C-4, page C-22).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby requires IV fluid:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Establish an IV line (page P-21), and give only IV fluid (according

to Table F-6) for the first 24 hours;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Give expressed breast milk using an alternative feeding method

(page C-14) every three hours starting on day 2, or later if the baby’s condition is not yet stable, and slowly decrease the volume of IV fluid while increasing the volume of oral feeds according to Table F-6. TABLE F-6 Volumes of IV fluid and breast milk for a sick baby weighing 1.75 kg to 2.5 kg

Day of Life

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1; 2; 3; 4; 5; 6; 7

- Dòng dữ liệu: IV fluid rate (ml/hour or; 5; 4; 3; 2; 0; 0; 0

microdrops/minute)

### chapter for the baby’s problem), allow the baby to begin breastfeeding — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: Feed volume every three; 0; 6; 14; 22; 30; 35; 38

hours (ml/feed)

## 1.5 To 1.749 Kg

### 1.5 To 1.749 Kg — phần 1

• Establish an IV line (page P-21), and give only IV fluid (according to Table F-7) for the first 24 hours.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give expressed breast milk by gastric tube (page C-18) every three hours

starting on day 2, or later if the baby’s condition is not yet stable, and slowly decrease the volume of IV fluid while increasing the volume of oral feeds according to Table F-7.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Progress to feeding by cup/spoon (page C-16) as soon as the baby can

swallow without coughing or spitting. TABLE F-7 Volumes of IV fluid and breast milk for a sick baby weighing 1.5 to 1.749 kg Day of Life

hours (ml/feed)

## 1.25 To 1.49 Kg

### 1.25 To 1.49 Kg — phần 1

• Establish an IV line (page P-21), and give only IV fluid (according to Table F-8) for the first 24 hours.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give expressed breast milk by gastric tube (page C-18) every three hours

starting on day 2, or later if the baby’s condition is not yet stable, and slowly decrease the volume of IV fluid while increasing the volume of oral feeds according to Table F-8.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Progress to feeding by cup/spoon (page C-16) as soon as the baby can

swallow without coughing or spitting. TABLE F-8 Volumes of IV fluid and breast milk for a sick baby weighing 1.25 to 1.49 kg Day of Life

hours (ml/feed)
