## Skin-To-Skin Contact

### Skin-To-Skin Contact — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Clothe and secure the baby as described for kangaroo mother care (page

F-30).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the temperature of the room where the rewarming takes place

is at least 25 °C.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Measure the baby’s body temperature two hours after beginning skin-to-

skin contact unless directed otherwise by another chapter. If the baby’s temperature is not 36.5 °C to 37.5 °C after the two hours of rewarming, reassess the baby (Table F-2, page F-11).

## Radiant Warmer

### Radiant Warmer — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the temperature of the room where the radiant warmer (Fig.

C-1, page C-6) is used is at least 22 °C.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Clean the mattress and platform, and cover the mattress with a clean

linen sheet.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-6; Maintaining normal body temperature

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-1; Radiant warmer

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Turn on the warmer and set the temperature according to the

manufacturer’s instructions (usually between 36 °C and 37.5 °C). When it is known beforehand that a baby is to arrive in the newborn special care unit, turn on the warmer to pre-warm the linen and mattress so that the baby does not initially lie on a cold surface.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the baby’s head is covered and the baby is clothed or covered

unless it is necessary for the baby to be naked or partially undressed for observation or a procedure.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place only one baby under each radiant warmer.

### Radiant Warmer — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Turn the baby frequently while under the warmer, if possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby is receiving IV fluid or expressed breast milk, increase the

volume of fluid and/or milk by 10% of the total daily volume per day (Table C-4, page C-22) for as long as the baby is under the radiant warmer.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Check the temperature of the warmer and of the room every hour, and

adjust the temperature setting accordingly.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Move the baby to be with the mother as soon as the baby no longer

requires frequent procedures and treatment.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Maintaining normal body temperature; C-7

## Incubator

### Incubator — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Determine the appropriate temperature for the incubator (Fig. C-2) based

on the baby’s weight and age (Table C-3).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Warm the incubator to the desired temperature before placing the baby

inside.

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-2; Incubator

- Dòng dữ liệu: TABLE C-3; Recommended incubator temperatures

- Dòng dữ liệu: Weight of Baby; Incubator Temperature by Agea

- Dòng dữ liệu: 35 °C; 34 °C; 33 °C; 32 °C

- Dòng dữ liệu: Less than 1.5 kg; 1 to 10 days; 11 days to 3; 3 to 5 weeks; More than

- Dòng dữ liệu: old; weeks old; old; 5 weeks old

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1.5 to 2.0 kg; 1 to 10 days; 11 days to; More than

- Dòng dữ liệu: old; 4 weeks old; 4 weeks old

- Dòng dữ liệu: 2.1 to 2.5 kg; 1 to 2 days; 3 days to; More than

- Dòng dữ liệu: old; 3 weeks old; 3 weeks old

- Dòng dữ liệu: More than 2.5 kg; 1 to 2 days; More than

- Dòng dữ liệu: old; 2 days old

### Incubator — phần 2

a If the incubator is single-walled, increase the incubator temperature 1 °C for every 7 °C difference in temperature between the room and the incubator.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-8; Maintaining normal body temperature

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Clean the mattress and cover it with a clean linen sheet.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the incubator’s water reservoir is empty; dangerous bacteria

may grow in the water and infect the baby. Leaving the reservoir dry will not affect the function of the incubator.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the baby’s head is covered and the baby is clothed or covered

unless it is necessary for the baby to be naked or partially undressed for observation or a procedure.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place only one baby in each incubator.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Close the hood as quickly as possible after placing the baby inside, and

keep the portholes of the incubator closed at all times to keep the incubator warm.

### Incubator — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Check the temperature of the incubator every hour for the first eight

hours, and then every three hours:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the temperature of the incubator does not match the set

temperature, the incubator may not be functioning properly; adjust the temperature setting until the desired temperature is reached inside the incubator, or use another method to warm the baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Measure the baby’s temperature (page P-5) every hour for the first eight

hours, and then every three hours:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby’s temperature is less than 36.5 °C or more than

37.5 °C, adjust the temperature of the incubator accordingly;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby’s temperature remains less than 36.5 °C or more

than 37.5 °C in spite of the incubator being kept at the recommended setting, manage for abnormal body temperature (page F-69).

### Incubator — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Move the baby to be with the mother as soon as the baby no longer

requires special care and frequent procedures and treatment.

## Warm Room

### Warm Room — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the baby’s head is covered and the baby is adequately clothed

or covered.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the temperature of the room is at least 26 °C. A warm room is

often uncomfortable for adult staff and caregivers; ensure that the baby is not neglected and that the staff and caregivers do not decrease the temperature without providing another warming method.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Maintaining normal body temperature; C-9

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place the baby in a cot in the room, away from cold walls and windows,

and away from draughts.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Measure the temperature of the room and the baby’s body temperature

four times daily.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide additional warmth at night.

## Other Methods

### Other Methods — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the baby’s head is covered and the baby is adequately clothed

or covered.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the object (e.g. hot water bottle or brick) does not come into

contact with the baby’s skin, which could cause burns.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the object is not too hot.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Monitor the warmth of the object and replace it before it becomes cold.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-10; Maintaining normal body temperature

## Feeding

### Feeding — phần 1

Ensure that the baby is fed as soon as possible after birth (within one hour if possible) or within three hours of admission unless feeding should be delayed because of a specific problem. If possible, admit the mother when admitting the baby. Encourage the mother to breastfeed the baby or to give expressed breast milk; support whichever method of feeding the mother chooses. Note that this guide assumes that the mother is available to breastfeed or express

breast milk for her baby. If the baby is small (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks gestation), review the general principles of feeding a small baby (page F-24).

## General Principles Of Exclusive Breastfeeding

### General Principles Of Exclusive Breastfeeding — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage early and exclusive breastfeeding whenever possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain to the mother and her family the benefits of early and exclusive

breastfeeding:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Breast milk contains the exact nutrients the baby needs and promotes

the baby’s development;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Breast milk is easily digested and efficiently used by the baby’s

body;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Breast milk protects the baby from infection;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Breastfeeding can be used as a contraceptive method (lactational

amenorrhoea method).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage the mother to breastfeed the baby on demand, both day and

night (eight or more times in 24 hours), for as long as the baby wants.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Have the mother offer the second breast once the baby releases the first

breast on her/his own.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Advise the mother that she should not:

### General Principles Of Exclusive Breastfeeding — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; force the baby to feed;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; interrupt a feed before the baby is done;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; use artificial teats or pacifiers;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; give the baby any other food or drink (e.g. commercial breast-milk

substitute, animal milks, local porridges, tea, water, etc.) other than breast milk for the first six months of life.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-12; Feeding and fluid management

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Include the mother’s partner, a family member, or other support person

in discussions about breastfeeding, if possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the mother eats nutritious food and has enough to drink.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the mother can wash or shower daily, but tell her to avoid

washing or wiping her nipples before breastfeeding.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain to the mother that most medications she may be given will not

### General Principles Of Exclusive Breastfeeding — phần 3

harm her baby while she breastfeeds; however, if the mother is taking cotrimoxazole or pyrimethamine with sulfadoxine, monitor the baby for jaundice.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the mother is HIV positive, see page F-159 for guidelines to help the

mother choose the most suitable feeding method.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the mother is too ill or if she chooses not to breastfeed:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Give the baby a breast-milk substitute (page C-19);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Advise the mother on how to care for her breasts:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Explain to the mother that her breasts may be uncomfortable for

some time, but she should avoid stimulating them. If the mother’s discomfort is severe, she can express a small amount of milk (page C-15) a few times a day to relieve the discomfort;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Advise the mother to support her breasts with a well-fitting bra

### General Principles Of Exclusive Breastfeeding — phần 4

or cloth, but she should not bind the breasts tightly because this may increase her discomfort;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Suggest that the mother apply a warm or cold compress to her

breasts to reduce swelling.

## Correct Positioning And Attachment For Breastfeeding

### Correct Positioning And Attachment For Breastfeeding — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask the mother to help the baby attach when the baby seems to be ready.

Signs of readiness to suckle include opening the mouth, rooting or searching, looking around, and moving.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain to the mother how to hold her baby during breastfeeding. She

should:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; hold the baby in skin-to-skin contact (page C-5), if possible;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; hold the baby’s head and body straight so that the baby faces her

breast, with the baby’s nose near her nipple;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; support the baby’s whole body, not just the neck and shoulders.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Feeding and fluid management; C-13

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain to the mother how to encourage her baby to attach (Fig. C-3).

She should:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; touch the baby’s lips with her nipple;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; wait until the baby’s mouth is opening wide;

### Correct Positioning And Attachment For Breastfeeding — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; move the baby quickly onto her breast, so that the baby’s lower lip is

well below the nipple. FIGURE C-3 Encouraging the baby to attach to the breast

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Assess attachment on the breast and suckling. Help the mother if she

wishes, especially if she is a first time or very young mother. Signs of correct attachment (Fig. C-4, page C-14) are:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; baby’s chin touches the breast;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; baby’s mouth is wide open with the lower lip curled out;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; more of the areola is visible above than below the mouth;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; baby suckles with slow, deep sucks and pauses sometimes.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the mother’s breasts are engorged, have her express a small amount

of breast milk (page C-15) before beginning to breastfeed. This will soften the area around the nipple so that it is easier for the baby to attach.

### Correct Positioning And Attachment For Breastfeeding — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-14; Feeding and fluid management

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-4; Correct (A) and incorrect (B) attachment to the breast

## Breastfeeding Twins

### Breastfeeding Twins — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Reassure the mother that she has enough breast milk for both babies.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the babies are small (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks

gestation), review the general principles of feeding a small baby (page F-24). In addition, have the mother:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; begin feeding one baby at a time until breastfeeding is well

established in both babies;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; ensure that the weaker twin gets enough milk;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; rotate at every breastfeeding the breast each baby is offered;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; give expressed breast milk using an alternative feeding method

(below) after the initial breastfeed, if necessary.

## Feeding The Baby Using An Alternative Feeding Method

### Feeding The Baby Using An Alternative Feeding Method — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Teach the mother how to express breast milk, if necessary (page C-15).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage the mother to express breast milk at least eight times in 24

hours.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Assess feeding ability twice daily, and encourage and support the mother

to begin breastfeeding as soon as the baby shows signs of readiness to suckle unless treatment of the baby’s illness prevents breastfeeding (e.g. the baby is receiving oxygen).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Record the following each time the baby is fed:

- Dòng dữ liệu: Feeding and fluid management; C-15

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; time of feeding;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; amount and kind of milk given (e.g. expressed breast milk or breast-

milk substitute);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; any feeding difficulty.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Calculate the volume of milk required according to the baby’s age

(Table C-4, page C-22).

### Feeding The Baby Using An Alternative Feeding Method — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the baby is receiving enough milk by assessing the baby’s

growth (page C-53).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Choose the most appropriate alternative feeding method:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; cup, cup and spoon, or other device (page C-16);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; hand-expressing breast milk into the baby’s mouth (page C-18);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; gastric tube (page C-18).

## Expressing Breast Milk

### Expressing Breast Milk — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Teach the mother how to express breast milk herself. The mother should:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; obtain a clean (washed, boiled or rinsed with boiling water, and air-

dried) cup or container to collect and store the milk;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; wash her hands thoroughly;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; sit or stand comfortably and hold the container underneath her

breast;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; express the milk (Fig. C-5, page C-16):

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; support the breast with four fingers and place the thumb above

the areola;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; squeeze the areola between the thumb and fingers while

pressing backwards against the chest;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; express each breast for at least four minutes, alternating breasts

until the flow of milk stops (both breasts are completely expressed).

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-16; Feeding and fluid management

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-5; Expressing breast milk

### Expressing Breast Milk — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the milk does not flow well:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; ensure that the mother is using the correct technique;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; have the mother apply warm compresses to her breasts;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; have someone massage the mother’s back and neck.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the expressed breast milk is not going to be used immediately, label

the container and either refrigerate the milk and use within 24 hours or freeze the milk (if freezing conditions can be reliably maintained) at –20 °C for no more than six months:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If a refrigerator or freezer is not available, keep the milk covered

at room temperature for up to six hours;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Ensure that the milk is at room temperature before giving it to the

baby:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Warm frozen or refrigerated milk in a warm water bath

(approximately 40 °C), but avoid overheating the milk;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Use the rewarmed milk promptly.

## Feeding By Cup, Cup And Spoon, Or Other Device

### Feeding By Cup, Cup And Spoon, Or Other Device — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the mother can properly express breast milk (page C-15).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Feed the baby using a cup, a cup and spoon, or other suitable device (e.g.

paladai) (Fig. C-6, page C-17).

- Dòng dữ liệu: Feeding and fluid management; C-17

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use clean (washed, boiled or rinsed with boiled water, and air-dried)

utensils and feeding devices for each feed.

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-6; Feeding by cup (A), paladai (B), or cup and spoon (C)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Feed the baby immediately after the milk is expressed, if possible. If the

baby does not consume all of the milk, store the remaining milk according to the guidelines on page C-16 for expressed breast milk.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Have the mother feed the baby unless she is not available. The mother

should:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; measure the volume of breast milk in the cup, ensuring that it meets the

### Feeding By Cup, Cup And Spoon, Or Other Device — phần 2

required volume according to the baby’s age (Table C-4, page C-22);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; hold the baby sitting semi-upright on her lap;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; rest the cup (or paladai or spoon) lightly on the baby’s lower lip and

touch the outer part of the baby’s upper lip with the edge of the cup;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; tip the cup (or paladai or spoon) so the milk just reaches the baby’s

lips;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; allow the baby to take the milk; do not pour the milk into the baby’s

mouth;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; end the feeding when the baby closes her/his mouth and is no longer

interested in feeding.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby does not take the necessary volume of milk (according to

Table C-4, page C-22), have the mother encourage the baby to feed for a longer time or feed more often.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage the mother to begin breastfeeding as soon as the baby shows

signs of readiness to suckle.

### Feeding By Cup, Cup And Spoon, Or Other Device — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-18; Feeding and fluid management

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby is not feeding well using a feeding device or the mother

prefers not to use it, have the mother attempt to hand-express breast milk directly into the baby’s mouth (page C-18).

## Hand-Expressing Breast Milk Into Baby’S Mouth

### Hand-Expressing Breast Milk Into Baby’S Mouth — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the mother can properly express breast milk (page C-15).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Have the mother:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; hold the baby with the baby’s mouth close to her nipple;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; express the breast until some drops of milk appear on the nipple;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; let the baby smell the nipple and attempt to suck, and allow some

breast milk to fall into the baby’s mouth;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; express more drops of breast milk after the baby swallows;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; end the feeding when the baby closes her/his mouth and is no longer

interested in feeding.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask the mother to repeat this process every one to two hours if the baby

weighs less than 1.5 kg or every two to three hours if the baby weighs 1.5 kg or more.

## Feeding Expressed Breast Milk By Gastric Tube

### Feeding Expressed Breast Milk By Gastric Tube — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the mother can properly express breast milk (page C-15).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Insert a gastric tube (page P-33) if one is not already in place.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Confirm that the tube is properly positioned (page P-35) before each

feeding.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage the mother to hold the baby and participate in feedings.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Determine the required volume of milk for the feed according to the

baby’s age (Table C-4, page C-22).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Remove the plunger of a high-level disinfected or sterile syringe (of a

size large enough to hold the required volume of milk) and connect the barrel of the syringe to the end of the gastric tube:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If a high-level disinfected or sterile syringe is not available, use a

clean (washed, boiled or rinsed with boiled water, and air-dried) syringe;

### Feeding Expressed Breast Milk By Gastric Tube — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If a suitable syringe is not available, use any other suitable, clean

funnel that connects snugly to the gastric tube.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Feeding and fluid management; C-19

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Pour the required volume of milk for the feed into the syringe with the

“tip” of the syringe pointed downward.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Have the mother hold the syringe 5 to 10 cm above the baby (Fig. C-7)

or suspend the tube above the baby and allow the milk to run down the tube by gravity. Do not force milk through the tube using the plunger of the syringe.

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-7; Feeding expressed breast milk by gastric tube

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Using this method, each feeding should take 10 to 15 minutes. If the flow

of milk is too fast, slightly pinch the tube below the syringe to slow down the flow.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; When the feeding is finished, remove, wash, and high-level disinfect or

### Feeding Expressed Breast Milk By Gastric Tube — phần 3

sterilize the syringe, and cap the tube until the next feeding.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Progress to feeding by cup/spoon when the baby can swallow without

coughing or spitting milk. This could be in as little as one or two days, or it may take longer than one week.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Replace the gastric tube with another clean gastric tube after three days,

or earlier if it is pulled out or becomes blocked, and clean and high-level disinfect or sterilize it according to Table C-11 (page C-43).

## Breast-Milk Substitutes

### Breast-Milk Substitutes — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the mother cannot breastfeed or express breast milk, use a

commercial breast-milk substitute. (Note that the following instructions are for the health care provider. Ensure that the mother knows how to properly prepare the breast-milk substitute before discharging the baby.)

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-20; Feeding and fluid management

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby is small (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks

gestation), use a breast-milk substitute designed for premature or small babies.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Once a container of breast-milk substitute is open, use the substitute

within the recommended time according to the manufacturer’s instructions (e.g. use liquid breast-milk substitute within four hours of opening the container).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Check the expiry date of the breast-milk substitute.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use aseptic technique to prepare the breast-milk substitute from liquid

### Breast-Milk Substitutes — phần 2

concentrates or powders, using high-level disinfected or sterile utensils and containers, and sterilized or boiled and cooled water.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Wash hands with soap and water.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Determine the required volume of milk for the feed according to the

baby’s age (Table C-4, page C-22).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Measure the breast-milk substitute and water, mix them, and feed the

baby using a cup, cup and spoon, or other device (page C-16). Have the mother do this whenever possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Store remaining milk in a labelled container in a refrigerator for a

maximum of 24 hours.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If commercial breast-milk substitute is not available, have the mother

use a breast-milk substitute that is based on animal milk and prepared at home (see national guidelines for correct preparation).

## Fluid Management

### Fluid Management — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; IV fluid is given to ensure that the baby receives necessary fluid,

minimum calories, and electrolytes. Give IV fluid only if directed to do so in a chapter in the section Assessment, Findings, and Management.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby is small (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks

gestation), see page F-24 for special considerations for fluid and feeding management of small babies.

## Choice Of Iv Fluid

### Choice Of Iv Fluid — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give 10% glucose for the first three days of life.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Feeding and fluid management; C-21

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; On the fourth day of life, if urine output is well established, give 10%

glucose with 3 mmol/kg body weight of sodium and 2 mmol/kg body weight of potassium:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; It is preferable to use commercially-produced IV fluid, such as

glucose in quarter normal saline, if available. Using this fluid avoids the risk of introducing infection by having to add electrolytes and eliminates errors in calculations when making the solution;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If these premixed solutions are not available, add normal saline 20

### Choice Of Iv Fluid — phần 2

ml/kg body weight to the required volume of 10% glucose. To calculate the necessary fluid volume, determine the volume of fluid required for day of life (see Table C-4, page C-22) and add 20 ml/kg body weight of normal saline, but infuse only the required daily volume. For example:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; For a baby who is four days old, the required daily fluid volume

is 120 ml/kg body weight;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Add 20 ml/kg body weight of normal saline to the 120 ml/kg

body weight of 10% glucose for a total fluid volume of 140 ml/kg body weight;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Infuse 120 ml/kg body weight of this mixed solution.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Addition of potassium to IV fluid is required when a baby cannot be

fed for a prolonged period of time. This must be done with extreme caution because a small overdose can have serious consequences:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Commonly available preparations are 7.5% and 15% KCl,

### Choice Of Iv Fluid — phần 3

which contain 1 and 2 mmol of potassium per ml, respectively;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; While supplementing potassium, add 2 ml/kg body weight of

7.5% KCl or 1 ml/kg body weight of 15% KCl to the total volume of IV fluid infused each day.

## Administration Of Iv Fluid

### Administration Of Iv Fluid — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use an infusion set with a microdropper (where 1 ml = 60 microdrops).

Microdroppers allow slow administration of fluid and ensure that babies receive the volume of fluid they need. Using a standard infusion set (where 1 ml = 20 drops) can cause dangerous fluid overload.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Before infusing IV fluid, check:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; the expiry date of the fluid;

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-22; Feeding and fluid management

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; that the seal of the infusion bottle or bag is not broken;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; that the fluid is clear and free from visible particles.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Calculate the rate of administration, and ensure that the microdropper

delivers the fluid at the required rate.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Change the IV infusion set and fluid bag every 24 hours, even if the bag

still contains IV fluid (they can be a major source of infection).

## Volumes Of Fluid And Feeds During The First Days Of

### Volumes Of Fluid And Feeds During The First Days Of — phần 1

LIFE

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Determine the required volume of fluid according to the baby’s age

(Table C-4; for small babies [less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks gestation], see pages F-26 to F-30). Note that Table combines the volumes of IV fluid and feeds (day 1 is the day of birth).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Subtract the volume of feeds the baby is receiving from the total daily

volume required to determine the necessary volume of IV fluid.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Adjust the volume of feeds and/or fluid if directed to do so by another

chapter (e.g. if the baby is placed under a radiant warmer or is receiving phototherapy, increase the volume of feeds and/or fluid by 10% of the total daily volume per day because of increased loss of water from the skin).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Convert the total volume into ml per hour or drops per minute.

- Dòng dữ liệu: TABLE C-4; Total daily feed and fluid volumes for babiesa from birth

### Volumes Of Fluid And Feeds During The First Days Of — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: Day of Life; 1; 2; 3; 4; 5; 6; 7+

- Dòng dữ liệu: ml/kg body weight of; 60; 80; 100; 120; 140; 150; 160+

feeds and/or fluid a See pages F-26 to F-30 for fluid volumes for small babies (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks gestation).

## Monitoring Babies Receiving Iv Fluid

### Monitoring Babies Receiving Iv Fluid — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Inspect the infusion site every hour:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Look for redness and swelling around the insertion site of the

cannula, which indicate that the cannula is not in the vein and fluid is leaking into the subcutaneous tissue. If redness or swelling is seen at any time, stop the infusion, remove the needle, and establish a new IV line (page P-21) in a different vein;

- Dòng dữ liệu: Feeding and fluid management; C-23

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Check the volume of fluid infused and compare to the prescribed

volume;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Record all findings.

Solutions containing glucose can cause tissue to die and should not be allowed to leak into subcutaneous tissue.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Measure blood glucose every six hours:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the blood glucose is less than 45 mg/dl (2.6 mmol/l), treat for

low blood glucose (page F-91);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the blood glucose is more than 105 mg/dl (6 mmol/l)

### Monitoring Babies Receiving Iv Fluid — phần 2

(hyperglycaemia) on two consecutive readings:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Change to a 5% glucose solution, if possible;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Measure blood glucose again in three hours.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Assess hydration daily:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If there are signs of dehydration (e.g. sunken eyes or fontanelle,

loss of skin elasticity, or dry tongue and mucous membranes), increase the volume of fluid by 10% of the baby’s body weight on the first day that the dehydration is noted;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If there are signs of overhydration (e.g. excessive weight gain,

puffy eyes, or increasing oedema over lower parts of the body), reduce the volume of fluid by half for 24 hours after the overhydration is noted.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Document in the baby’s clinical record when the baby urinates. If there is

little or no urine output in 24 hours in the absence of asphyxia, increase the volume of fluid by 10%, as for dehydration (above).

### Monitoring Babies Receiving Iv Fluid — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Weigh the baby daily. If the daily weight loss is more than 5%,

increase the total volume of fluid by 10 ml/kg body weight for one day to compensate for inadequate fluid administration.

## Combining Iv Fluid And Feeding

### Combining Iv Fluid And Feeding — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow the baby to begin breastfeeding as soon as the baby’s condition

improves unless directed otherwise by a chapter in the section Assessment, Findings, and Management. If the baby cannot be

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-24; Feeding and fluid management

breastfed, give expressed breast milk using an alternative feeding method (page C-14).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby tolerates the feed and there are no problems, continue to

increase the volume of feeds while decreasing the volume of IV fluid to maintain the total daily fluid volume according to the baby’s daily requirement (Table C-4, page C-22).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Feed the baby every three hours, or more frequently if necessary,

adjusting the volume at each feeding accordingly.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Add the total volume of feeds and fluid given each day. Compare this

volume with the required daily volume, and adjust the volume that the baby receives accordingly.

### Combining Iv Fluid And Feeding — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Discontinue the infusion of IV fluid when the baby is receiving more

than two-thirds of the daily fluid volume by mouth and has no abdominal distension or vomiting.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage the mother to breastfeed exclusively as soon as the baby is

receiving 100% of the daily fluid volume by mouth.

## Administering Oxygen

### Administering Oxygen — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Review the methods of administering oxygen, their instructions for use,

and advantages and disadvantages (Table C-5).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Continue to perform any examinations, tests, procedures, and treatment

while the baby is receiving oxygen.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the baby can tolerate oral feedings, give expressed breast milk by

gastric tube (page C-18). If the baby cannot be fed, establish an IV line (page P-21) and give IV fluid at maintenance volume according to the baby’s age (Table C-4, page C-22).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the baby does not receive too little or too much oxygen:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Giving too little oxygen may cause organ damage and eventual death;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Giving too much oxygen may damage the baby’s lungs and retinas.

### Administering Oxygen — phần 2

This damage, however, occurs after days (rather than minutes or hours) of excess oxygen therapy and is unlikely to occur in babies more than 35 weeks gestation.

## Nasal Prongs

### Nasal Prongs — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use 1-mm prongs for a small baby (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born

before 37 weeks gestation) and use 2-mm prongs for a term baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place the prongs just within the baby’s nostrils.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Secure the prongs in place using elastic or a piece of adhesive tape.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Oxygen therapy; C-27

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Adjust the flow of oxygen to achieve the desired concentration.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Change the nasal prongs twice daily. Give oxygen using a face mask

(page C-28) while cleaning and disinfecting the prongs (see Table C-11, page C-43), if necessary.

## Nasal Catheter

### Nasal Catheter — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use an 8-F catheter. If the 8-F catheter is too large, use a 6-F catheter.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Determine the distance the tube should be passed by measuring the

distance from the nostril to the inner margin of the eyebrow.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Gently insert the catheter into the nostril. If a gastric tube is already in

place in one nostril, insert the catheter into the same nostril that the gastric tube is in, if possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the catheter is correctly positioned:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Look into the baby’s mouth;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; The catheter should not be visible at the back of the mouth;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the catheter is visible at the back of the mouth, pull the catheter

out slowly until it is no longer visible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Adjust the flow of oxygen to achieve the desired concentration.

### Nasal Catheter — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Change the nasal catheter twice daily. Give oxygen using a face mask

(page C-28) while cleaning and disinfecting the catheter (see Table C-11, page C-43), if necessary.

## Head Box

### Head Box — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place a head box (Fig. C-8, page C-28) over the baby’s head.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the baby’s head stays within the head box, even when the

baby moves.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Adjust the flow of oxygen to achieve the desired concentration.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-28; Oxygen therapy

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-8; Baby receiving oxygen via a head box

## Face Mask

### Face Mask — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place the mask over the baby’s mouth and nose.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Secure the mask in place using elastic or a piece of adhesive tape.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Adjust the flow of oxygen to achieve the desired concentration.

## Incubator

### Incubator — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use a head box, following the instructions for a head box (page C-27),

or connect the oxygen directly to the incubator according to the manufacturer’s instructions.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Adjust the flow of oxygen to achieve the desired concentration.

## Sources Of Oxygen

### Sources Of Oxygen — phần 1

Ensure that a source of oxygen (Table C-6, page C-29) is available at all times. Oxygen is expensive, so use it only in situations where it is necessary, and discontinue as soon as possible. There are three main sources of oxygen, which are described below. The oxygen is carried from the source to the baby by means of non-crush, plastic oxygen delivery tubing. A face mask, which can give a high concentration of oxygen, should always be available in case

of rapid deterioration of the baby’s condition.

## Monitoring The Baby’S Response To Oxygen

### Monitoring The Baby’S Response To Oxygen — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use an oximeter according to the manufacturer’s instructions to ensure

that the baby receives an adequate concentration of oxygen.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If an oximeter is not available, monitor the baby for signs of

oxygenation by assessing whether the baby has signs of breathing difficulty or central cyanosis (blue tongue and lips) (note that these observations cannot differentiate between normal and excessive concentrations of oxygen in the blood):

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-30; Oxygen therapy

Central cyanosis is a late sign that the baby is not receiving enough oxygen. If the baby shows signs of central cyanosis, increase the concentration of oxygen immediately and continue until cyanosis is eliminated.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the breathing difficulty is moderate to severe (see Table F-12,

page F-49), give oxygen at a moderate flow rate;

### Monitoring The Baby’S Response To Oxygen — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; When the baby’s breathing begins to improve (e.g. respiratory rate

begins to move towards the normal range, grunting or chest indrawing decreases), decrease the oxygen flow;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; When the baby’s respiratory rate is within the normal range and

there are no other signs of breathing difficulty (e.g. chest indrawing or grunting on expiration), remove oxygen and observe the baby for 15 minutes:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby’s tongue and lips remain pink, do not give any

more oxygen. Observe for central cyanosis every 15 minutes for the next hour;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If central cyanosis reappears at any time, give oxygen again

at the last rate given;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Continue to observe the baby for 24 hours after oxygen is

discontinued.

## Choice And Administration Of Antibiotics

### Choice And Administration Of Antibiotics — phần 1

Antibiotic choice begins empirically with the selection of the drug(s) that is most likely to be effective against the organism causing the baby’s illness. If bacterial culture and sensitivity are possible, the results of these investigations guide further treatment, particularly if the baby is not responding to the empirically chosen antibiotic(s). If bacterial culture and sensitivity are not possible and the baby is not

responding to the initial antibiotic(s), a second-line antibiotic or combination of antibiotics is given on an empiric basis. Table C-7 (page C-32) lists the first- line antibiotic(s) for the infections described in this guide and the second-line antibiotic(s) to use if the baby shows no improvement (by the third day, unless directed otherwise by a chapter in the section Assessment, Findings, and Management) and if culture and sensitivity have not been performed. Review

### Choice And Administration Of Antibiotics — phần 2

Table C-8 (page C-33) for information on diluting antibiotics, and see Table C-9 (pages C-34 to C-35) for antibiotic doses according to the baby’s age. Note that the information provided in this chapter applies only to antibiotics given to the baby. For information regarding dosage of antibiotics for the mother and/or her partner (e.g. for a sexually transmitted infection), see the appropriate chapter in the section Assessment, Findings, and Management.

## Route Of Administration

### Route Of Administration — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; For sepsis, meningitis, tetanus, and congenital syphilis, give antibiotics

intravenously (IV) (page P-17):

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Give the antibiotics, especially gentamicin and cefotaxime, slowly

over a period of at least three minutes;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Monitor the baby while giving IV fluids (page C-22) to ensure that

fluid overload does not occur;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If an IV line cannot be established immediately, give antibiotics

intramuscularly (IM) (page P-15) until an IV line is in place;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; When the baby is recovering and an IV line is no longer needed for

another purpose, give the antibiotics IM to complete the course of treatment.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; For most other infections, give the antibiotics IM (page P-15). If an IV

line is needed for another purpose, however, give the antibiotics IV.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-32; Antibiotics

- Dòng dữ liệu: benzylpenicillin; (100 000; (100 000; once daily; once daily

- Dòng dữ liệu: for congenital; units/kg); units/kg)

- Dòng dữ liệu: syphilis without; once daily; once daily

CNS involvement

- Dòng dữ liệu: Procaine; 100 mg/kg; 100 mg/kg; 0.5 ml/kg in a 0.5 ml/kg in a

- Dòng dữ liệu: benzylpenicillin; (100 000; (100 000; single dose; single dose

- Dòng dữ liệu: for asymptomatic units/kg) in a; units/kg) in a

- Dòng dữ liệu: baby of mother; single dose; single dose

### Route Of Administration — phần 10

not treated for syphilis a See pages C-31 to C-33 for information on route of administration and directions for diluting antibiotics.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-36; Antibiotics

## Infection Prevention C-37

### Infection Prevention C-37 — phần 1

Infection prevention is an important part of every component of care of a newborn baby. Newborn babies are more susceptible to infections because their immune system is immature; thus, the consequences of failing to follow infection prevention principles are particularly devastating. Key infection prevention practices are summarized below.

## General Principles Of Infection Prevention

### General Principles Of Infection Prevention — phần 1

Observing the infection prevention practices below will protect the baby, mother, and health care provider from infections. They also will help prevent the spread of infections.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide routine care of the newborn baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Consider every person (including the baby and staff) as potentially

infectious.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Wash hands or use an alcohol-based handrub.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Wear protective clothing and gloves.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use aseptic technique.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Handle sharp instruments carefully, and clean and, if necessary, sterilize

or disinfect instruments and equipment.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Routinely clean the newborn special care unit, and dispose of waste.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Isolate babies with infections to prevent nosocomial infections.

## Provide Routine Care Of The Newborn Baby

### Provide Routine Care Of The Newborn Baby — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; After the first six hours of life or after the baby’s temperature is stable,

use cotton cloths soaked in warm water to remove blood and other body fluids (e.g. from the birth) from the baby’s skin, and then dry the skin. Delay bathing a small baby (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks gestation) until at least the second day of life.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Clean the buttocks and perineal area of the baby each time the baby’s

napkin is changed, or as often as required, using cotton soaked in warm, soapy water, and then carefully dry the area.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-38; Infection prevention

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the mother knows correct positioning and attachment for

breastfeeding (page C-12) to prevent mastitis and nipple damage.

## People As Sources Of Infection

### People As Sources Of Infection — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Locate the newborn special care unit in a low-traffic area with restricted

access.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Have a private room for newborn babies, if possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that staff in direct contact with newborn babies receive as many

of the following immunizations as possible:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; rubella;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; measles;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; hepatitis B virus;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; mumps;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; influenza (yearly).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not allow personnel with skin infections or lesions to come into direct

contact with babies.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not allow staff or visitors to enter the newborn special care unit if

they have an acute infection (e.g. respiratory virus).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Limit the number of different individuals handling the baby.

## Handwashing

### Handwashing — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Wash hands with soap and water (if hands are visibly clean, disinfect

them using an alcohol-based handrub):

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; before and after caring for a baby and before any procedure;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; after removing gloves;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; after handling soiled instruments or other items.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Instruct the mother and family members to wash their hands before and

after handling the baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; To wash hands:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Thoroughly wet hands;

- Dòng dữ liệu: Infection prevention; C-39

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wash hands for 10 to 15 seconds with plain soap and running or

poured water;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Allow hands to air-dry or dry them with a clean paper or personal

towel.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; An alcohol-based handrub, made from adding 2 ml of glycerine (or other

### Handwashing — phần 2

emollient) to 100 ml of 60% to 90% ethyl or isopropyl alcohol, is more effective in cleaning hands than handwashing unless the hands are visibly soiled. To clean hands using an alcohol-based handrub:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Apply enough handrub to cover the entire surface of hands and

fingers;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Rub the solution into hands until they are dry.

## Protective Clothing And Gloves

### Protective Clothing And Gloves — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; It is not necessary to wear gowns or masks when providing routine care

for newborn babies.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Wear protective clothing (e.g. aprons, gowns) when contact with blood

or body fluids is anticipated.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Wear closed-toe shoes, if possible. Do not go barefoot.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; When gloves are required for a procedure, wear a separate pair of gloves

for each baby to avoid cross-contamination, and dispose of gloves after contact. Use different gloves for different situations:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wear sterile or high-level disinfected gloves for contact with broken

skin or for invasive procedures (e.g. lumbar puncture, umbilical vein catheterization);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wear clean examination gloves for contact with mucous membranes

or body fluids (e.g. taking a blood sample, caring for the umbilicus);

### Protective Clothing And Gloves — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wear heavy rubber or latex utility gloves for handling contaminated

items, cleaning instruments and equipment, and disposing of waste.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Disposable gloves are preferred, but where resources are limited, gloves

can be reused if they are:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; decontaminated by soaking in 0.5% chlorine solution for 10 minutes;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; washed and rinsed;

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-40; Infection prevention

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; sterilized by autoclaving (eliminates all microorganisms) or high-

level disinfected by steaming or boiling (eliminates all microorganisms except some bacterial endospores).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If single-use disposable surgical gloves are reused, do not process

them more than three times because invisible tears may occur.

Do not use gloves that are cracked or peeling or have visible holes or tears.

## Aseptic Technique

### Aseptic Technique — phần 1

Using aseptic technique eliminates or reduces to a safe level the number of microorganisms on skin, tissue, and inanimate objects.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Scrub hands for three to five minutes using an antiseptic soap, and rinse

with running or poured water.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow hands to air dry or dry them with a clean paper or personal towel.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Put on clean examination gloves.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Prepare the skin for procedures by washing with a swab or cotton-wool

ball soaked in an antiseptic solution (e.g. Table C-10, page C-41) in an outward spiral motion. Repeat two more times, using a new swab or cotton-wool ball each time, and allow to dry. If polyvidone iodine is used, allow it to dry after applying or wait at least two minutes before continuing with the procedure.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Remove examination gloves and put on high-level disinfected or sterile

gloves.

### Aseptic Technique — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use sterile or high-level disinfected instruments and equipment.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If there is any question about whether an item is sterile or not,

consider it contaminated.

## Multi-Use Vials

### Multi-Use Vials — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use a new, sterile needle and syringe each time medication is withdrawn

from a multi-use vial or container.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Store multi-use vials according to their instructions (e.g. store in a dark,

cool area or refrigerate).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Record on the vial the date and time it was opened, and use for up to one

month or according to the expiry date.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Infection prevention; C-41

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not keep opened glass ampules so that the drug can be used for

multiple babies. The drug may not be stable, and taping ampules shut will not prevent contamination.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Discard diluent solutions (e.g. sterile water or normal saline) after 24

hours.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Change the IV infusion set and fluid bag every 24 hours, even if the bag

still contains IV fluid (they can be a major source of infection).

## Antiseptic And Disinfectant Solutions

### Antiseptic And Disinfectant Solutions — phần 1

Although the terms are sometimes used interchangeably, antiseptic and disinfectant solutions (Table C-10) serve different purposes. Antiseptic solutions are used on the skin and are usually not as strong as disinfectants. Disinfectant solutions are used to decontaminate or high-level disinfect instruments and equipment.

- Dòng dữ liệu: TABLE C-10; Acceptable antiseptic and disinfectant solutions

- Dòng dữ liệu: Acceptable Antiseptic Solutions; Acceptable Disinfectant Solutionsa

- Dòng dữ liệu: • 2.5% polyvidone iodine (for skin; • 0.5% chlorine bleach (for

- Dòng dữ liệu: preparation or scrub); decontamination of surfaces and high-

- Dòng dữ liệu: • 4% chlorhexidine gluconate (for skin; level disinfection of instruments)

- Dòng dữ liệu: preparation or scrub); • 2% glutaraldehyde

### Antiseptic And Disinfectant Solutions — phần 2

• 60% to 90% ethyl or isopropyl alcohol (for skin preparation for taking a blood sample or establishment of an IV line) a Do not use disinfectants with phenolic compounds, as they may harm newborn babies.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; To prevent contamination of antiseptic and disinfectant solutions:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; use only boiled water for dilution, if dilution is necessary (boil water

for 20 minutes to high-level disinfect it);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; take care not to contaminate the mouth of the container when

pouring the solution into smaller containers;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; empty and wash containers with soap and water and allow them to

air dry at least once weekly;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; pour the antiseptic solution onto cotton-wool balls or gauze. Do not

dip into the solution;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; store the solutions in a cool, dark place.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-42; Infection prevention

## Safe Handling Of Sharp Instruments

### Safe Handling Of Sharp Instruments — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; After use, decontaminate syringes and needles by flushing them with a

disinfectant solution (Table C-10, page C-41) three times.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Immediately dispose of sharps by placing them in a puncture-proof

container. Do not recap, bend, or break the needle or remove it from the syringe. If the needle must be recapped, use the one-handed recap method:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Place the cap on a hard, flat surface;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Hold the syringe with one hand and use the needle to “scoop up” the

cap;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; When the cap covers the needle completely, hold the base of the

needle and use the other hand to secure the cap.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Dispose of the container as directed on page C-44.

## Instrument Processing

### Instrument Processing — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; See Table C-11 (page C-43) for specific guidelines for processing

instruments to ensure that they are clean and high-level disinfected or sterile.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that instruments that penetrate the skin (e.g. needles, catheters)

are adequately sterilized or high-level disinfected before use and properly processed after use.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use a disinfectant solution to wipe off equipment that does not contact

the bloodstream (e.g. stethoscope, incubator) between each use, and particularly between use with different babies.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Infection prevention; C-43

- Dòng dữ liệu: TABLE C-11; Guidelines for processing instruments and equipment

- Dòng dữ liệu: Instrument; Processing Guidelines (after each use)

- Dòng dữ liệu: Thermometers and stethoscopes; • Wipe with a disinfectant solution

- Dòng dữ liệu: Resuscitation bag and mask; • Wipe exposed surfaces with gauze pad soaked in

### Instrument Processing — phần 2

disinfectant solution • Wash with soap and water

- Dòng dữ liệu: Incubator or radiant warmer; • Wipe with a disinfectant solution daily

• Wash radiant warmer with soap and water before using for a new baby • Wash incubator weekly, if the same baby is still in the incubator, and before using for a new baby

- Dòng dữ liệu: Suction apparatus and catheter,; • Soak in disinfectant solution for 10 minutes

- Dòng dữ liệu: gastric tube, nasal prongs, nasal; • Wash with soap and water

- Dòng dữ liệu: catheter, syringes; • High-level disinfect or sterilize

- Dòng dữ liệu: Oxygen head box; • Wash with soap and water

## Housekeeping And Waste Disposal

### Housekeeping And Waste Disposal — phần 1

Regular and thorough cleaning will decrease microorganisms on surfaces and help prevent infection. Remember the following about housekeeping and waste disposal:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Every newborn special care unit should have a housekeeping schedule:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Post the cleaning schedule in a visible area (see Table C-12, page

C-44 for a sample cleaning schedule);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Provide details on exactly what needs to be done and how often;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Educate staff regarding cleaning, and delegate responsibility.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Follow general guidelines for housekeeping:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Clean from the top to the bottom (e.g. of walls and window

coverings) so that the dirt that falls during cleaning is removed;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Always wear heavy rubber or latex utility gloves;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Ensure that a fresh bucket containing disinfectant solution is

available at all times;

### Housekeeping And Waste Disposal — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-44; Infection prevention

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Immediately clean up spills of blood or body fluids using

disinfectant solution;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wrap or cover clean linens and store them in an enclosed cart or

cabinet to prevent contamination with dust;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; After each use, wipe off beds, tables, and procedure trolleys using

disinfectant solution.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Separate contaminated waste (e.g. items soiled by blood, pus, and other

body fluids) from non-contaminated waste.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use a puncture-proof container for contaminated sharps, and destroy the

container when two-thirds full:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Add a small amount of kerosene to the container, and burn the waste

in an open area downwind from the service site;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If it is not possible to burn the disposal container, bury it in a site

at least 50 metres away from a water source.

### Housekeeping And Waste Disposal — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: TABLE C-12; Sample cleaning schedule for the newborn special care unit

- Dòng dữ liệu: Frequency; Cleaning Guidelines

- Dòng dữ liệu: Daily; • Wet-mop floors with a disinfectant and detergent solution. Do not

sweep floors or use cleaning methods that increase dust. • Wipe incubators and radiant warmers with a disinfectant solution.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Between; • Wipe equipment, bassinets, examination tables, etc., with a cloth

- Dòng dữ liệu: Babies; dampened with disinfectant solution.

• Clean incubators and radiant warmers between each use, including the mattress, with a disinfectant solution. Allow the incubator to dry completely before placing a baby inside.

- Dòng dữ liệu: As Needed; • Clean windows, walls, lamps, chairs, and window coverings to

### Housekeeping And Waste Disposal — phần 4

prevent accumulation of dust. • Remove and destroy or clean contaminated waste containers. • Remove and destroy the sharps-disposal container and replace with another suitable container. • Clean spills of blood or body fluids with a disinfectant solution.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Infection prevention; C-45

## Additional Methods To Prevent Infection

### Additional Methods To Prevent Infection — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Have a private room specifically for newborn babies, if possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Avoid overcrowding and understaffing.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not place two or more babies in the same cot or incubator or under

the same radiant warmer or phototherapy unit.

## Nosocomial Infections

### Nosocomial Infections — phần 1

Nosocomial infections are infections that are acquired in the health care facility. A presumptive epidemic in a newborn special care unit is defined as finding two or more babies with the same condition (e.g. skin or eye infection, infectious diarrhoea) at the same time. If a nosocomial infection occurs, strict control measures should be put in place and monitored to resolve the problem. For skin and eye infections and diarrhoea (the most

common nosocomial infections in newborn special care units), perform the following:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Isolate the baby by placing the baby and her/his mother in a private

room:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Leave the door open, if necessary, to ensure that the baby and

mother are not neglected;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If a private room is not available, place all babies with the same

infection, but no other infections, in the same room.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; When entering the room with the baby:

### Nosocomial Infections — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wear clean examination gloves, and change gloves after contact

with infectious material (e.g. faecal material, gauze used to wash pustules or blisters);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wear a clean gown if contact with the baby or infectious material is

anticipated.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Before leaving the room:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Remove the gown;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Remove gloves;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Wash hands with an antibacterial soap or alcohol-based handrub;

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-46; Infection prevention

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Avoid touching potentially contaminated surfaces or objects, and

ensure that clothing does not come in contact with potentially contaminated surfaces or objects.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Restrict transfer of the baby to other areas of the health care facility

unless absolutely necessary. During transfer, maintain infection prevention precautions.

### Nosocomial Infections — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Reserve non-critical care equipment (e.g. stethoscope, thermometer) for

use only with the infected baby, if possible, and carefully clean and disinfect equipment shared among infected and non-infected babies.

## Clinical Use Of Blood C-47

### Clinical Use Of Blood C-47 — phần 1

Blood is transfused only for a condition that cannot be managed effectively by other means. District health care facilities should be prepared for the urgent need for blood transfusion. Health care facilities with newborn special care units should keep stored blood available, especially type O, Rh-negative blood. Splitting units of blood into smaller bags (e.g. 50 ml) suitable for babies can help prevent wasting blood and reduces the risk of transfusing too

much blood. For the procedure of transfusing blood, see page P-31.

## Principles Of Blood Transfusion

### Principles Of Blood Transfusion — phần 1

Principles of blood transfusion to remember include the following:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Transfusion is only one element of managing a problem in a baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The baby’s haemoglobin level (or haematocrit), although important,

should not be the only deciding factor in giving a transfusion. The decision to transfuse should be supported by the need to relieve clinical signs (e.g. active and ongoing bleeding) and prevent significant morbidity and mortality. Note that the measured haemoglobin will not reflect the baby’s actual clinical condition in cases of ongoing bleeding.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; In small babies (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks

gestation), the haemoglobin may gradually fall (anaemia of prematurity), but transfusion is recommended only when the haemoglobin is less than 8 g/dl (haematocrit less than 24%) if the baby is otherwise well.

### Principles Of Blood Transfusion — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The volume of blood taken for laboratory tests should be minimized to

decrease “wastage” of the baby’s blood to reduce the need for transfusion. Use of special low-volume collection tubes, if available, is helpful.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If blood is not immediately available for transfusion, give crystalloid

fluid (e.g. normal saline or Ringer’s lactate) until blood can be obtained.

## Reducing Risks Of Blood Transfusion

### Reducing Risks Of Blood Transfusion — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The transfusion of blood carries the risk of:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; viral infections, such as HIV and hepatitis;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; bacterial infections (any blood product can become contaminated

with bacteria if it is prepared or stored incorrectly);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; serious haemolytic transfusion reactions;

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-48; Clinical use of blood

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; graft-versus-host disease.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The risks associated with transfusion can be reduced by:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; effectively selecting, deferring, and excluding blood donors;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; screening for transfusion-transmissible infections in the blood donor

population (e.g. HIV and hepatitis; see below);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; ensuring high quality blood-grouping, compatibility testing, storage,

and transportation of blood;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; ensuring that the blood bank follows recommendations for safe

blood;

### Reducing Risks Of Blood Transfusion — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; appropriately using blood;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; establishing and maintaining quality assurance programmes.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Blood that is given to the baby must be cross-matched to the blood of

both the mother and the baby. When sending the baby’s blood sample for blood-typing and cross-matching, always send a sample of the mother’s blood, if possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; In areas with a high prevalence of malaria, give prophylactic antimalarial

drugs to the baby receiving the blood.

## Screening Blood For Infectious Agents

### Screening Blood For Infectious Agents — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Screen every unit of donated blood for transfusion-transmissible

infections, using the most appropriate and effective tests, in accordance with both national policies and the prevalence of infectious agents in the potential blood donor population.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; All donated blood should be screened for:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; HIV-1 and HIV-2;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; hepatitis B surface antigen (HbsAg);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Treponema pallidum antibody (syphilis).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where possible, all donated blood should also be screened for:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; hepatitis C;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Chagas disease (in countries where the seroprevalence is high);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; cytomegalovirus;

- Dòng dữ liệu: Clinical use of blood; C-49

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; malaria (in low-prevalence countries when donors have travelled to

malarial areas).

### Screening Blood For Infectious Agents — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; No blood should be released for transfusion until all nationally required

tests are shown to be negative.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Perform compatibility tests on all blood transfused even if, in life-

threatening emergencies, the tests are performed after the blood has been issued.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-50; Clinical use of blood

## General Guidelines

### General Guidelines — phần 1

The following guidelines are general and should be harmonized with existing national policies.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Vaccinate the baby at birth against tuberculosis (where prevalence is

high), poliomyelitis, and hepatitis B unless directed otherwise below.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Immunize the baby according to the following guidelines, regardless of

whether the baby:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; is small (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks gestation).

Immunize at the usual age (chronological age and not corrected age), and do not reduce vaccine dose;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; has been hospitalized for a prolonged period of time. If the baby is

still in the hospital at 60 days of age, complete a first course of immunization (described below) and also give diphtheria, pertussis, and tetanus (DPT) vaccine 0.5 ml IM in the upper thigh before the baby is discharged;

### General Guidelines — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; has a clinically stable neurologic condition (e.g. brain injury);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; was born to a mother who is HIV positive;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; is receiving treatment with antibiotics;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; has jaundice.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Be sure to use a sterile or high-level disinfected syringe and needle for

each immunization and for each baby.

## Tuberculosis Vaccine (Bcg)

### Tuberculosis Vaccine (Bcg) — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give a single dose of 0.05 ml intradermally (page P-19) in the upper

portion of the left arm using a special BCG syringe.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; In countries with a high prevalence of tuberculosis, give the BCG

immunization as soon after birth as possible, with the following exceptions:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby is sick, give the immunization after the baby has

recovered and just before discharge from the hospital;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby’s mother has active lung tuberculosis and has been

treated for less than two months before birth or is diagnosed with tuberculosis after birth, see page F-155.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-52; Immunization

## Poliomyelitis Vaccine (Opv)

### Poliomyelitis Vaccine (Opv) — phần 1

There is no risk of nosocomial spread of polio as a result of immunizing babies with OPV.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place two drops of the vaccine on the baby’s tongue.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give four doses of OPV for effective protection:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; In polio-endemic areas, give a single dose of OPV at birth or up to

two weeks after birth;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Regardless of whether the first dose was given at birth, give the

other three doses of OPV at 6, 10, and 14 weeks, respectively.

## Hepatitis B Vaccine (Hbv)

### Hepatitis B Vaccine (Hbv) — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give HBV (paediatric formulation) 0.5 ml IM in the upper thigh (page

P-15). Note that the efficacy may be lower if the vaccine is injected into the buttocks.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give three doses of HBV:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the baby is sick, give the first dose as soon as the baby has

recovered;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the mother is known to be HbsAg positive or if perinatal

transmission is common:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Give the first dose shortly after birth (preferably within 12 hours

of birth);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Give the second and third doses at 6 and 14 weeks, respectively.

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the mother is known to be HbsAg negative and the baby is still

in the hospital at 60 days of age, give HBV just before the baby is discharged;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; In all other cases, give the first dose at six weeks and give the

second and third doses at an interval at least four weeks apart.

## General Principles

### General Principles — phần 1

The most commonly used method for monitoring and assessing growth is weight gain. Until breastfeeding is established, or if the baby is sick or small (less than 2.5 kg at birth or born before 37 weeks gestation), the baby may not gain, or may even lose, weight. Babies weighing 1.5 to 2.5 kg may lose up to 10% of their birth weight in the first four to five days after birth, and babies weighing less than 1.5 kg may lose up to 15% of their birth weight during the first 7 to 10 days after birth.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Unless the baby requires immediate drugs or fluid based on body weight,

weigh the baby at birth after the baby’s temperature has stabilized or upon admission to:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; identify low birth weight and anticipate problems associated with it;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; have a reference value for monitoring growth;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; calculate drug doses and volume of fluid to give, if necessary;

### General Principles — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; assess the adequacy of food and fluid intake.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Weigh and assess weight gain twice weekly (note that weighing on the

same two days each week establishes a routine that is easy to follow) until the baby is gaining weight for three consecutive assessments, and then weigh weekly for as long as the baby is in the hospital (unless directed to do so more frequently in another chapter):

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; A minimum daily weight gain of 15 g/kg body weight per day over

three days is desirable after the initial period of weight loss;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; After birth weight has been regained, weight gain during the first

three months of feeding should be:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; 150 to 200 g per week for babies weighing less than 1.5 kg (i.e.

20 to 30 g per day);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; 200 to 250 g per week for babies weighing 1.5 to 2.5 kg (i.e. 30

to 35 g per day).

## Weighing Technique

### Weighing Technique — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use a precise and accurate scale, with 5- or 10-g increments, made

especially for weighing babies.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-54; Assessing growth

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Adjust/standardize the scale according to the manufacturer’s instructions.

If the manufacturer’s instructions are not available, adjust the scale weekly or whenever the scale is moved.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place a clean cloth/paper in the weighing pan.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Adjust the scale to zero with the cloth/paper in the pan.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Place the naked baby gently on the cloth/paper.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Wait for the baby to settle and the weight to stabilize.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Read the weight to the nearest 5 or 10 g.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Record the weight in the baby’s record and plot it on the weight chart

(below).

## Recording Weight

### Recording Weight — phần 1

Figure C-9 (page C-55) is a blank weight chart that can be used to monitor the weight of a sick or small baby. On the horizontal axis are spaces to record the number of days after admission. The vertical axis is for the weight in kilograms, stepped in 100-g increments and marked in 500-g increments, but the exact weight has been left blank so that the chart can be used for any baby irrespective of the starting weight. If the baby’s birth weight is known, mark it as day 0. Mark the admission day

and fill in the starting weight at the appropriate level (e.g. 1.5, 2.0, 2.2 kg). Ensure that enough space is left on the vertical axis of the chart to plot initial weight loss. Chart the baby’s weight while hospitalized, and calculate the weight gain/loss. See Fig. C-10 (page C-56) for an example of a completed chart.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Assessing growth; C-55

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-9; Blank weight chart

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-56; Assessing growth

### Recording Weight — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: FIGURE C-10; Example of a completed weight chart

## Communication And Emotional Support C-57

### Communication And Emotional Support C-57 — phần 1

Emergency situations are often very disturbing for everyone involved and evoke a range of emotions that can have significant consequences. The need for the baby, whether sick or small, to be in the unfamiliar environment of a health care facility is a stressful and emotional experience for the family, especially the mother. In addition to the family’s fear of the baby dying, they may have feelings of guilt, anger, and denial.

## General Principles Of Communication

### General Principles Of Communication — phần 1

When communicating with the mother and family, remember the following:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Be respectful and understanding.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Listen to the family’s concerns and encourage them to ask questions and

express their emotions.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use simple and clear language when giving the family information about

the baby’s condition, progress, and treatment, and ensure that the family understands what you have told them. If you do not speak a language the family understands, use a sensitive translator.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Respect the family’s right to privacy and confidentiality.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Respect the family’s cultural beliefs and customs, and accommodate the

family’s needs as far as possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that the family understands any instructions, and, if possible, give

written information to family members who can read.

### General Principles Of Communication — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Obtain informed consent before performing procedures, if possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Remember that health care providers may feel anger, guilt, sorrow, pain,

and frustration. Showing emotion is not a weakness.

## Emotional And Psychological Reactions

### Emotional And Psychological Reactions — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; How each member of the family reacts to an emergency situation may

depend on the:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; marital status of the mother and her relationship with her partner;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; social situation of the mother/couple and their cultural and religious

practices, beliefs, and expectations;

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-58; Communication and emotional support

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; personalities of the people involved and the quality and nature of

social and emotional support;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; nature, gravity, and prognosis of the problem and the availability and

quality of the health care services;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; anticipated costs of health care.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Common reactions to newborn problems or death include:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; denial (feelings of “it can’t be true”);

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; guilt regarding possible responsibility;

### Emotional And Psychological Reactions — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; anger, which is frequently directed towards health care providers and

staff but often masks anger that parents direct at themselves for their “failure”;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; bargaining, particularly if the baby has a life-threatening condition;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; depression and loss of self-esteem, which may be long-lasting;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; isolation (feelings of being different or separate from others), which

may be reinforced by health care providers who avoid people who experience loss;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; disorientation.

## Emotional Support For The Family

### Emotional Support For The Family — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not place blame on the family if there is a question of neglect or

intervening too late.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow the parents to take a photograph of the baby, if they wish to. This

can provide great comfort to the parents, especially if they are not able to visit the baby very often.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Show that you care about the baby and the family and that you respect

them:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Express your feelings of concern for the family and encourage them

to express their emotions, if culturally appropriate;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Encourage the mother to stay with her baby, and admit her, if

necessary. If the mother is not able to stay with the baby, encourage her to visit the baby as often as she wishes. Ensure that there are sufficient rooms available nearby so that she can establish and maintain breastfeeding and be with her baby;

- Dòng dữ liệu: Communication and emotional support; C-59

### Emotional Support For The Family — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Encourage the mother to take an active role in the care of her baby

and simple procedures, if possible;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Encourage the mother to touch and hold the baby as much and as

often as she wants;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the mother is alone, identify a friend or a particular member of

the staff to support her;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Provide extra support and encouragement to a mother who is

expressing breast milk for a sick or small baby.

## Visitation

### Visitation — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow and encourage visits by close family members and friends

whenever possible:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; If the number of visitors must be limited, choose one person to act

as the representative and report to the others;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Encourage the family to visit the baby, but do not insist. Family

members may be reluctant to visit the baby for fear of what they will see or because they do not want to become too attached to the baby until they know if the baby will survive;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; Do not equate the frequency of visiting with the quality of the baby-

parent bond or level of concern for the baby. Practical problems—the cost of travel, the need to care for other children at home, the mother’s need for medical treatment at a different hospital, or responsibilities of employment or farming—can prevent the mother and other family members from visiting.

### Visitation — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow the baby’s sibling(s) to visit the baby unless the sibling has a

fever, signs of acute illness (e.g. acute respiratory infection), or recent exposure to a communicable disease (e.g. chickenpox).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ensure that a visiting child:

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; has contact only with her/his sibling;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; has properly washed her/his hands;

- Dòng dữ liệu: -; is supervised at all times.

- Dòng dữ liệu: C-60; Communication and emotional support

## Defect

### Defect — phần 1

The birth of a baby with a neurodevelopmental problem (e.g. brain injury from birth asphyxia or bilirubin encephalopathy) or a birth defect can be a devastating experience for the parents and family. Reactions may vary. Having a baby with obvious abnormalities is very distressing for parents, and in many societies this is associated with particular stigma for the mother. Different families have different responses and needs, and the health care staff cannot use
